Zheng Shi and Umang Mathur are partially supported by the National Research Foundation, Singapore, and Cyber Security Agency of Singapore under its National Cybersecurity R&D Programme (Fuzz Testing <NRF-NCR25-Fuzz-0001>). Any opinions, findings and conclusions, or recommendations expressed in this material are those of the author(s) and do not reflect the views of National Research Foundation, Singapore, and Cyber Security Agency of Singapore.
Lasse Møldrup and Andreas Pavlogiannis were partially supported by a research grant (VIL42117) from VILLUM FONDEN, and by a research grant from STIBOFONDEN.

Appendix A
Proofs for Section
3

A.1.
Proof for Section
3.1

See
3.1

Proof.

For convenience, for a node
v=\langle Y,Q,I\rangle
in
G_{\mathsf{frontier}}
,
we use
v.Y,v.Q,v.I
to denote
Y,Q,I
.
We prove each direction separately.

Correctness(Reachability
\Rightarrow
Consistency).

We now show if there exists a sink node
v=\langle\mathsf{S},Q,\bot\rangle
for some
Q
and
v
is reachable from the source node
v^{\prime}=\langle\varnothing,\lambda~\mathtt{ch}.\epsilon,\bot\rangle
in
G_{\mathsf{frontier}}
,
then there is a concretization of
\langle\mathcal{X},\mathsf{cap}\rangle
.
Let
\pi
be a path from
v^{\prime}
to
v
in
G_{\mathsf{frontier}}
.
We directly give out the concretization
\sigma
of
\langle\mathcal{X},\mathsf{cap}\rangle
as the sequence of labelling events corresponding to
\pi
.

We now show
\sigma
is indeed a valid concretization.
Firstly,
\mathsf{Events(\sigma)}=\mathsf{S}
,
because each edge
v_{1}\xrightarrow{e}v_{2}
guarantees
e\notin v_{1}.Y
and
v_{2}.Y=v_{1}.Y\cup\{e\}
.
Since we start from
v^{\prime}.Y=\varnothing
and end at
v.Y=\mathsf{S}
,
the path
\pi
must contain all events in
\mathsf{S}
.
Therefore, we have
\mathsf{Events(\sigma)}=\mathsf{S}
.

Secondly,
\sigma
satisfies
po
,
otherwise if
e_{1}<^{\sigma}_{\mathsf{\textsf{tr}}}e_{2}
and
e_{2}
is program ordered before
e_{1}
,
then the event set
Y
extended by
e_{1}
is not
po
-closed,
which violates our definition for the node.

Thirdly, every receive operation should observe a send operation with the same value,
and this property is already captured when we define the edges of
G_{\mathsf{frontier}}
.

Lastly,
\sigma
should meet the capacity constraints.
For synchronous channels,
we already guarantee that no events can execute between and send and the its corresponding receiver on a synchronous channel,
because send and receive to any channel can execute only when
I=\bot
,
so that it’s impossible for send or receive events on other synchronous channels to interleave.
The capacity constraints for asynchronous channels are also met,
because when we define the edges of
G_{\mathsf{frontier}}
,
an arbitrary node
u=\langle Y_{u},Q_{u},I_{u}\rangle
can only be extended by a send event on channel
\mathtt{ch}
,
if the number of buffered send events to
\mathtt{ch}
in
Y_{u}
is below
\mathsf{cap}\mathsf{(\mathtt{ch})}
.
With all these observations combined,
\sigma
is indeed a correct concretization.

Correctness(Consistency
\Rightarrow
Reachability).

We now show if there is a concretization
\sigma
of
\langle\mathcal{X},\mathsf{cap}\rangle
,
then there exists a sink node
v=\langle\mathsf{S},Q,\bot\rangle
for some
Q
and
v
is reachable from the source node
v^{\prime}=\langle\varnothing,\lambda~\mathtt{ch}.\epsilon,\bot\rangle
in
G_{\mathsf{frontier}}
.
We can start from the source node
v^{\prime}
,
and in the
i
-th step,
we just extend current node by an edge,
which is labelled by the
i
-th event in
\sigma
.
By the definition of edges in the frontier graph,
every step of extension is allowed.
Moreover, since
\sigma
is a valid concretization,
then
\mathsf{Events(\sigma)}=\mathsf{S}
and thus this path ends at a node whose event set is exactly
\mathsf{S}
.
Therefore, the correctness is guaranteed.
∎

A.2.
Proof for Section
3.2

See
3.3

Proof.

We prove each direction separately.

Consistency
\Rightarrow
Acyclicity.

Suppose
G_{\mathsf{sync}}
has a cycle, then
\langle\mathcal{X},\mathsf{cap},{\textsf{rf}}\rangle
is not consistent.
We assume there is a cycle in
G_{\mathsf{sync}}
,
which contains two nodes
\langle\mathtt{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.8}snd}_{1},\mathtt{\color[rgb]{0,0.4,0}rcv}_{1}\rangle
and
\langle\mathtt{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.8}snd}_{2},\mathtt{\color[rgb]{0,0.4,0}rcv}_{2}\rangle
.
In any concretization
\sigma
of
\langle\mathcal{X},\mathsf{cap},{\textsf{rf}}\rangle
,
we must have
\mathtt{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.8}snd}_{1}<^{\sigma}_{\mathsf{\textsf{tr}}}\mathtt{\color[rgb]{0,0.4,0}rcv}_{1}<^{\sigma}_{\mathsf{\textsf{tr}}}\mathtt{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.8}snd}_{2}<^{\sigma}_{\mathsf{\textsf{tr}}}\mathtt{\color[rgb]{0,0.4,0}rcv}_{2}
,
because there is a path from
\langle\mathtt{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.8}snd}_{1},\mathtt{\color[rgb]{0,0.4,0}rcv}_{1}\rangle
to
\langle\mathtt{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.8}snd}_{2},\mathtt{\color[rgb]{0,0.4,0}rcv}_{2}\rangle
.
Similarly, we have
\mathtt{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.8}snd}_{2}<^{\sigma}_{\mathsf{\textsf{tr}}}\mathtt{\color[rgb]{0,0.4,0}rcv}_{2}<^{\sigma}_{\mathsf{\textsf{tr}}}\mathtt{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.8}snd}_{1}<^{\sigma}_{\mathsf{\textsf{tr}}}\mathtt{\color[rgb]{0,0.4,0}rcv}_{1}
,
because there is a path from
\langle\mathtt{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.8}snd}_{2},\mathtt{\color[rgb]{0,0.4,0}rcv}_{2}\rangle
to
\langle\mathtt{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.8}snd}_{1},\mathtt{\color[rgb]{0,0.4,0}rcv}_{1}\rangle
.
No total order
<^{\sigma}_{\mathsf{\textsf{tr}}}
can satisfy both requirements.

Acyclicity
\Rightarrow
Consistency.

Suppose
G_{\mathsf{sync}}
is acyclic.
Consider an arbitrary topological sort
\pi=\langle\mathtt{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.8}snd}_{1},\mathtt{\color[rgb]{0,0.4,0}rcv}_{1}\rangle\cdot\langle\mathtt{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.8}snd}_{2},\mathtt{\color[rgb]{0,0.4,0}rcv}_{2}\rangle

\cdots\langle\mathtt{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.8}snd}_{k},\mathtt{\color[rgb]{0,0.4,0}rcv}_{k}\rangle
of
G_{\mathsf{sync}}

and using it, define
\sigma=\mathtt{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.8}snd}_{1}\cdot\mathtt{\color[rgb]{0,0.4,0}rcv}_{1}\cdots\mathtt{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.8}snd}_{k}\cdot\mathtt{\color[rgb]{0,0.4,0}rcv}_{k}
.
We will show that this
\sigma
defined is a concretization of
\langle\mathcal{X},\mathsf{cap},{\textsf{rf}}\rangle
.
First,
\sigma
respects
rf
, because a send event is immediately followed by its receiver.
We now prove it also satisfies
po
.
Assume on the contrary that this is not the case.
Then, there are two events
e,e^{\prime}
, s.t.
(e,e^{\prime})\in{\textsf{po}}
,
but
e^{\prime}<^{\sigma}_{\mathsf{\textsf{tr}}}e
.
First,
e
and
e^{\prime}
cannot be matching send-receive events since
they belong to the same thread.
Let
e_{\mathtt{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.8}snd}}
be either
e
if
\mathsf{op}(e)=\mathtt{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.8}snd}
, and
{\textsf{rf}}(e)
otherwise,
and let
e_{\mathtt{\color[rgb]{0,0.4,0}rcv}}={\textsf{rf}}(e_{\mathtt{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.8}snd}})
.
Likewise, let
e^{\prime}_{\mathtt{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.8}snd}}
be either
e^{\prime}
if
\mathsf{op}(e^{\prime})=\mathtt{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.8}snd}
, and
{\textsf{rf}}(e^{\prime})
otherwise,
and let
e^{\prime}_{\mathtt{\color[rgb]{0,0.4,0}rcv}}={\textsf{rf}}(e^{\prime}_{\mathtt{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.8}snd}})
.
Also, by virtue of how
\sigma
was constructed, there is no path from
\langle e_{\mathtt{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.8}snd}},e_{\mathtt{\color[rgb]{0,0.4,0}rcv}}\rangle

to
\langle e^{\prime}_{\mathtt{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.8}snd}},e^{\prime}_{\mathtt{\color[rgb]{0,0.4,0}rcv}}\rangle
in
G_{\mathsf{sync}}
; or else we will have
e<^{\sigma}_{\mathsf{\textsf{tr}}}e^{\prime}
.
But this is a contradiction since
G_{\mathsf{sync}}
must add an edge
from
\langle e_{\mathtt{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.8}snd}},e_{\mathtt{\color[rgb]{0,0.4,0}rcv}}\rangle
to
\langle e_{\mathtt{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.8}snd}},e_{\mathtt{\color[rgb]{0,0.4,0}rcv}}\rangle
because
(e,e^{\prime})\in{\textsf{po}}
.
∎

A.3.
Proof for Section
3.3

See
3.4

Proof.

We prove each direction separately.

Correctness (
\langle\mathcal{X},\mathsf{cap},{\textsf{rf}}\rangle

\Rightarrow

\langle{\mathcal{X}}{\downharpoonright}_{\tau_{i},\tau_{j}},{\mathsf{cap}}{\downharpoonright}_{\tau_{i},\tau_{j}},{{\textsf{rf}}}{\downharpoonright}_{\tau_{i},\tau_{j}}\rangle
).

If
\langle\mathcal{X},\mathsf{cap},{\textsf{rf}}\rangle
is consistent,
then
\langle{\mathcal{X}}{\downharpoonright}_{\tau_{i},\tau_{j}},{\mathsf{cap}}{\downharpoonright}_{\tau_{i},\tau_{j}},{{\textsf{rf}}}{\downharpoonright}_{\tau_{i},\tau_{j}}\rangle
must be consistent for any
({\tau_{i},\tau_{j}})\in E
.
Otherwise, assuming
\langle{\mathcal{X}}{\downharpoonright}_{\tau_{i},\tau_{j}},{\mathsf{cap}}{\downharpoonright}_{\tau_{i},\tau_{j}},{{\textsf{rf}}}{\downharpoonright}_{\tau_{i},\tau_{j}}\rangle
is not consistent,
any concretization
\sigma
of
\langle\mathcal{X},\mathsf{cap},{\textsf{rf}}\rangle
will not be consistent,
because
\sigma
is not a valid concretization for thread
{\tau_{i},\tau_{j}}
.
It contradicts with the fact that
\langle\mathcal{X},\mathsf{cap},{\textsf{rf}}\rangle
is consistent.

Correctness (
\langle{\mathcal{X}}{\downharpoonright}_{\tau_{i},\tau_{j}},{\mathsf{cap}}{\downharpoonright}_{\tau_{i},\tau_{j}},{{\textsf{rf}}}{\downharpoonright}_{\tau_{i},\tau_{j}}\rangle

\Rightarrow

\langle\mathcal{X},\mathsf{cap},{\textsf{rf}}\rangle
).

Let
G
be the topology graph of the input
VCh-rf
instance.
To construct the concretization
\sigma
for
\langle\mathcal{X},\mathsf{cap},{\textsf{rf}}\rangle
,
we define a graph
G^{\prime}=\langle V^{\prime},E^{\prime}\rangle
,
where
V^{\prime}
is the set of all events in
\mathsf{S}
.
We have
(e_{1},e_{2})\in E^{\prime}
,
iff
(e_{1},e_{2})\in{\textsf{po}}
or
x_{e_{1},e_{2}}
exists in a 2SAT formula for some thread
{\tau_{1},\tau_{2}}
and
x_{e_{1},e_{2}}
is assigned true.
We claim
G^{\prime}
is acyclic and any linearization of
G^{\prime}
is a valid concretization.

If
G^{\prime}
is cyclic, then we pick an arbitrary cycle
C
.
Assuming the size of
C
is
r
,
then let
C=e_{c_{1}}\rightarrow\dots\rightarrow e_{c_{r}}\rightarrow e_{c_{1}}
.
We look at the threads of events in
C
,
i.e.
\mathsf{th}(e_{c_{1}}),\dots,\mathsf{th}(e_{c_{r}}),\mathsf{th}(e_{c_{1}})
.
Clearly, each pair of adjacent threads in this sequence shares at least one common channel.
Since the topology graph
G
is acyclic,
we claim there are at most two distinct threads among the threads of all events in
C
,
as otherwise,
G
has a cycle.
All events in the same thread are already ordered by
po
,
so that
C
cannot contain events only from one thread.
Therefore,
C
contains exactly two threads (say
\tau_{1},\tau_{2}
).
Note that
e_{i}\rightarrow e_{j}
is an edge in
G^{\prime}
iff
e_{i},e_{j}
are either in the same thread or access one of the common channels between
\mathsf{th}(e_{i}),\mathsf{th}(e_{j})
.
This means
e_{c_{1}},\dots,e_{c_{r}}
all access the common channels between
{\tau_{1},\tau_{2}}
.
However, this contradicts with the fact that
\langle{\mathcal{X}}{\downharpoonright}_{\tau_{1},\tau_{2}},{\mathsf{cap}}{\downharpoonright}_{\tau_{1},\tau_{2}},{{\textsf{rf}}}{\downharpoonright}_{\tau_{1},\tau_{2}}\rangle
is consistent,
so that
G^{\prime}
must be acyclic.

Now we show an arbitrary linearization
\sigma
of
G^{\prime}
is a valid concretization.

\sigma
respects
po
and
rf
,
because if
(e_{1},e_{2})\in({\textsf{po}}\cup{\textsf{rf}})
, then
(e_{1},e_{2})
must be an edge in
G^{\prime}
,
so that
e_{1}<^{\sigma}_{\mathsf{\textsf{tr}}}e_{2}
.
The capacity constrains,
FIFO
property are already taken care of in each sub-instance,
because a channel is at most accessed by two threads.
Therefore,
\sigma
is indeed a valid concretization of
\langle\mathcal{X},\mathsf{cap},{\textsf{rf}}\rangle
and it is indeed consistent.
∎

See
3.5

Proof.

We prove each direction separately.

Correctness (Satisfiability
\Rightarrow
Consistency).

Now assuming there
\varphi_{\langle\mathcal{X},\mathsf{cap},{\textsf{rf}}\rangle}
can be satisfied,
then the input
VCh-rf
instance
\langle\mathcal{X},\mathsf{cap},{\textsf{rf}}\rangle
is consistent and we sketch one concretization
\sigma
as following.
For every event pair
(e,f)
,
if
x_{e,f}
is true, then we order
e
before
f
in
\sigma
.
Firstly,
\sigma
is indeed a linear trace,
because
\varphi_{\textsf{exactly-1}}
guarantees
x_{e,f}=\neg x_{f,e}
,
so that for events from different thread,
we have a unique assignment for their relative ordering in
\sigma
.
Also,

\varphi_{\textsf{trans}}
guarantees that the transitivity of events orderings is taken care of.
That is if
e_{1}<^{\sigma}_{\mathsf{\textsf{tr}}}e_{2}
and
e_{2}<^{\sigma}_{\mathsf{\textsf{tr}}}e_{3}
,
then
e_{1}<^{\sigma}_{\mathsf{\textsf{tr}}}e_{3}
.
To see this, we enumerate all 4 possible situations.

(1)

\mathsf{th}(e_{1})=\mathsf{th}(e_{2})=\mathsf{th}(e_{3})
.
This implies
(e_{1},e_{2}),(e_{2},e_{3})\in{\textsf{po}}
,
so that
(e_{1},e_{3})\in{\textsf{po}}
and thus
e_{1}<^{\sigma}_{\mathsf{\textsf{tr}}}e_{3}
.

(2)

\mathsf{th}(e_{1})=\mathsf{th}(e_{2})\neq\mathsf{th}(e_{3})
.
Since
(e_{1},e_{2})\in{\textsf{po}}
,

\varphi_{{\textsf{po}}}
guarantees
e_{1}<^{\sigma}_{\mathsf{\textsf{tr}}}\mathsf{pred}(e_{2})
,
and
\varphi_{\textsf{trans}}
guarantees
\mathsf{pred}(e_{2})<^{\sigma}_{\mathsf{\textsf{tr}}}e_{3}
.
Therefore,
e_{1}<^{\sigma}_{\mathsf{\textsf{tr}}}e_{3}
.

(3)

\mathsf{th}(e_{1})\neq\mathsf{th}(e_{2})=\mathsf{th}(e_{3})
.
Since
(e_{2},e_{3})\in{\textsf{po}}
,

\varphi_{{\textsf{po}}}
guarantees
\mathsf{succ}(e_{2})<^{\sigma}_{\mathsf{\textsf{tr}}}e_{3}
,
and
\varphi_{\textsf{trans}}
guarantees
e_{1}<^{\sigma}_{\mathsf{\textsf{tr}}}\mathsf{succ}(e_{2})
.
Therefore,
e_{1}<^{\sigma}_{\mathsf{\textsf{tr}}}e_{3}
.

(4)

\mathsf{th}(e_{1})=\mathsf{th}(e_{3})\neq\mathsf{th}(e_{2})\neq
.
Since
e_{2}<^{\sigma}_{\mathsf{\textsf{tr}}}e_{3}
,
by transitivity we have

e_{2}<^{\sigma}_{\mathsf{\textsf{tr}}}\mathsf{succ}(e_{3})
.
If
e_{3}<^{\sigma}_{\mathsf{\textsf{tr}}}e_{1}
,
then
(e_{3},e_{1})\in{\textsf{po}}
,
and we would have
e_{2}<^{\sigma}_{\mathsf{\textsf{tr}}}e_{3}<^{e_{1}}_{\mathsf{\textsf{tr}}}
,
which contradicts with the fact that
e_{1}<^{\sigma}_{\mathsf{\textsf{tr}}}e_{2}
.

Therefore,
\sigma
cannot be cyclic.

Secondly,
the capacity constraints are also met,
because a channel
\mathtt{ch}
is capacity-unbounded, capacity 1 or capacity 0.
If
\mathtt{ch}
is unbounded,
then the capacity constraints are already satisfied
and
\varphi_{\textsf{cap=1}},\varphi_{\textsf{sync}}
are designed to satisfy the capacity constraints for channels with capacity 1 or 0.

Now we argue
\sigma
also respects
po
and
rf
.

\sigma
respects
po
,
because if
(e_{1},e_{2})\in{\textsf{po}}
,
then
x_{e_{1},e_{2}}
must be true,
so that
e_{1}
appears earlier than
e_{2}
in
\sigma
.
On the other hand,
\sigma
respects
rf
, because
\varphi_{{\textsf{rf}}}
orders all send events before their receive events.

\varphi_{\textsf{FIFO}}
guarantees for each channel
\mathtt{ch}
,
\mathtt{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.8}snd}_{1}(\mathtt{ch})
is before
\mathtt{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.8}snd}_{2}(\mathtt{ch})
, iff
\mathtt{\color[rgb]{0,0.4,0}rcv}_{1}(\mathtt{ch})
is before
\mathtt{\color[rgb]{0,0.4,0}rcv}_{2}(\mathtt{ch})
,
where
(\mathtt{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.8}snd}_{i}(\mathtt{ch}),\mathtt{\color[rgb]{0,0.4,0}rcv}_{i}(\mathtt{ch}))\in{\textsf{rf}}
for
i=1,2
.
Therefore,
rf
is also satisfied.
We have so far proved
\sigma
is indeed a concretization of
\langle\mathcal{X},\mathsf{cap},{\textsf{rf}}\rangle
and therefore
\langle\mathcal{X},\mathsf{cap},{\textsf{rf}}\rangle
is consistent.

Correctness (Consistency
\Rightarrow
Satisfiability).

If
\langle\mathcal{X},\mathsf{cap},{\textsf{rf}}\rangle
is consistent,
then we pick an arbitrary concretization
\sigma
.
We assign
x_{e,f}
to be true and
x_{f,e}
to be false,
iff in
\sigma
,
e
is ordered before
f
.
We now show this assignment satisfies
\varphi_{\langle\mathcal{X},\mathsf{cap},{\textsf{rf}}\rangle}
.
Firstly,
\varphi_{\textsf{exactly-1}}
is obviously satisfied,
because we guarantee
x_{e,f}=\neg x_{f,e}

by our assignments.
Secondly,
\varphi_{{\textsf{po}}}
and
\varphi_{{\textsf{rf}}}
are satisfied,
because
\sigma
must respect
\varphi_{{\textsf{po}}}
and
rf
relation.
Thirdly,
\sigma
guarantees the
FIFO
property of each channel
\mathtt{ch}
,
and for each channel
\mathtt{ch}
,

\sigma
orders all send events to
\mathtt{ch}
with no receivers after all send events to
\mathtt{ch}
with a receiver.
Otherwise, these unmatched send events will block the channel.
Therefore,
\varphi_{\textsf{FIFO}}
and
\varphi_{\textsf{unmatched}}
are satisfied.

\varphi_{\textsf{trans}}
is also satisfied,
because if
e\leq_{\mathsf{tr}}^{\sigma}f
, then
(1)
e^{\prime}=\mathsf{pred}(e)
is ordered before
f
,
and (2)
f^{\prime}=\mathsf{succ}(f)
is ordered after
e
,
otherwise,

\sigma
is not a linear trace.
Finally,
\varphi_{\textsf{cap=1}}
and
\varphi_{\textsf{sync}}
are satisfied,
because
\sigma
satisfies the capacity constraints.
∎

Appendix B
Lower bounds of
VCh

We now turn our attention to the hardness of
VCh
.
In Section
4.1
, we introduce atomicity gadgets, which is a construction to ensure a sequence of events to be executed atomically, and will be frequently used in later sections.
In Section
B.1
we prove Theorem
1.1
, namely that the problem is intractable even when all send/receive events use the same value.
In Section
B.2
we prove Theorem
1.2
, stating that hardness for
VCh
arises already with
2
threads, and even if there are no capacity constraints on the channels.
Finally, in Section
B.3
we prove Theorem
1.3
, showing that the problem is also hard already with
1
channel.

B.1.
Hardness with Same Values

We consider the
VCh
problem for instances where all events (no matter what channel they access) send/receive the same value.
We remark that, in the case of shared memory, this problem is known to be solvable in linear time —- simply check if, for each memory location
x
, there is some thread that writes to
x
before reading from it.
In the case of channels,
FIFO
and capacity constraints turn this problem intractable, as we prove here.

Overview.

Our proof is via a reduction from the Hamiltonian cycle problem on an directed graph
G
.
Given
G
with
{n_{v}}
nodes, we construct a
VCh
instance
\langle\mathcal{X},\mathsf{cap}\rangle

which is consistent iff
G
has a Hamiltonian cycle.
In high level,
\langle\mathcal{X},\mathsf{cap}\rangle
is constructed so that any concretization of
\langle\mathcal{X},\mathsf{cap}\rangle
can be conceptually split into three phases, based on the following scheme.
The initial phase picks an arbitrary node
v_{1}
as the start of the Hamiltonian cycle,
and also sends
{n_{v}}
messages to a channel, which act as a counter to keep track of the length of the Hamiltonian cycle constructed in the next phase.
The second phase guesses the Hamiltonian cycle edge-by-edge while decrementing the counter and also ensuring no node repeats.
The last phase executes residual send/receive events and verifies that the sequence of edges guessed in the second phase is indeed a Hamiltonian cycle.
See Figure
14
for an illustration on a small example.

u
v
w

A graph
G
with Hamiltonian cycle
u\to v\to w\to u

.

A graph
G
with a Hamiltonian cycle (left) and the corresponding
VCh
instance in which all send/receive events use the same value (right).
A concretization is
\sigma=[{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\tau_{\mathsf{init}}}]\cdot[{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\tau_{u,v}}\cdot{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\tau_{v,w}}\cdot{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\tau_{w,u}}]\cdot[{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\tau_{\mathsf{free}}}\cdot{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\tau_{u}}\cdot{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\tau_{v}}\cdot{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\tau_{w}}\cdot{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\tau_{w,v}}
],
obtained by fully executing every thread according to this sequence.
Brackets separate the three phases.

Reduction.

We now make the above idea formal.
Let
G=(V,E)
be the instance of the Hamiltonian cycle problem with
{n_{v}}
nodes and
{n_{e}}
edges.
We construct
VCh
instance
\langle\mathcal{X},\mathsf{cap}\rangle
that uses

{n_{e}}+{n_{v}}+2
threads
\{\tau_{(u,v)}\,|\,(u,v)\in E\}\cup\{\tau_{v}\,|\,v\in V\}\cup\{\tau_{\sf init},\tau_{\sf free}\}
, and
2{n_{v}}+3
channels
\{\mathtt{ch}_{v},\mathtt{ch}^{\prime}_{v}\,|\,v\in V\}\cup\{\ell,\alpha,{\sf cnt}\}
,
with the following capacities:

\mathsf{cap}\mathsf{(\mathtt{ch}_{v})}=\mathsf{out}(v)+\mathsf{in}(v)
,
\mathsf{cap}\mathsf{(\mathtt{ch}^{\prime}_{v})}=\mathsf{in}(v)
,

\mathsf{cap}\mathsf{({\sf cnt})}={n_{e}}
,
\mathsf{cap}\mathsf{(\ell)}=1
,
\mathsf{cap}\mathsf{(\alpha)}={n_{v}}
.
We use
\mathsf{out}(v)
and
\mathsf{in}(v)
to denote the out-degree and in-degree of a node
v\in V
.
For each
v\in V
, the sequence of events in thread
\tau_{v}
comprises
\mathsf{out}(v_{i})
blocks.

\displaystyle\tau_{v}=\mathtt{\color[rgb]{0,0.4,0}rcv}(\alpha)\cdot A^{1}_{v}\cdots A^{\mathsf{out}(v)}_{v}

The
j^{\text{th}}
block
A^{j}_{v}
encodes the
j^{\text{th}}
outgoing edge
(v,w)
from
v
.

\displaystyle A^{j}_{v}=\mathtt{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.8}snd}(\mathtt{ch}_{v})\cdot\mathtt{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.8}snd}(\mathtt{ch}^{\prime}_{w})\cdot\mathtt{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.8}snd}({\sf cnt})

For each edge
(u,v)\in E
, the sequence of events in
\tau_{(u,v)}
is:

\displaystyle\tau_{(u,v)}=\mathtt{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.8}snd}(\mathsf{\ell})\cdot\mathtt{\color[rgb]{0,0.4,0}rcv}(\mathtt{ch}_{u})\cdot\mathtt{\color[rgb]{0,0.4,0}rcv}({\sf cnt})\cdot\mathtt{\color[rgb]{0,0.4,0}rcv}(\mathtt{ch}^{\prime}_{v})\cdot\mathtt{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.8}snd}(\mathtt{ch}_{v})\cdot\mathtt{\color[rgb]{0,0.4,0}rcv}(\mathsf{\ell})

The thread
\tau_{\mathsf{i}nit}
sends a message to the channel
\mathtt{ch}_{v}
of a designated initial node
v
of the Hamiltonian cycle, sends
{n_{v}}
messages to the channel
{\sf cnt}
, and also sends one message to each channel
\{\mathtt{ch}^{\prime}_{u}\}_{u\in V}
.
After the Hamiltonian cycle has been constructed, the thread
\tau_{\mathsf{free}}
, together with
\{\tau_{u}\}_{u\in V}
empties all channels while ensuring that
v
is reached by a path that visits every node once.

\displaystyle\begin{array}[]{rcl}\tau_{\sf init}&=&\mathtt{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.8}snd}(\mathsf{\ell})\cdot\mathtt{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.8}snd}_{1}({\sf cnt})\cdots\mathtt{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.8}snd}_{{n_{v}}}({\sf cnt})\cdot\mathtt{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.8}snd}(\mathtt{ch}_{v_{1}})\cdot\mathtt{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.8}snd}(\mathtt{ch}^{\prime}_{v_{1}})\cdots\mathtt{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.8}snd}(\mathtt{ch}^{\prime}_{v_{n_{v}}})\cdot\mathtt{\color[rgb]{0,0.4,0}rcv}(\mathsf{\ell})\\
\tau_{\sf free}&=&\mathtt{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.8}snd}(\mathsf{\ell})\cdot\mathtt{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.8}snd}_{1}({\sf cnt})\cdots\mathtt{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.8}snd}_{{n_{e}}}({\sf cnt})\cdot\mathtt{\color[rgb]{0,0.4,0}rcv}(\mathtt{ch}_{v_{1}})\cdot\mathtt{\color[rgb]{0,0.4,0}rcv}_{1}({\sf cnt})\cdots\mathtt{\color[rgb]{0,0.4,0}rcv}_{{n_{e}}}({\sf cnt})\cdot\mathtt{\color[rgb]{0,0.4,0}rcv}(\mathsf{\ell})\\
&&\cdot\mathtt{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.8}snd}_{1}(\alpha)\cdots\mathtt{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.8}snd}_{{n_{v}}}(\alpha)\end{array}

The following lemma states the correctness of the construction.

Lemma B.1.

\langle\mathcal{X},\mathsf{cap}\rangle
is consistent iff
G
contains a Hamiltonian cycle.

Finally, observe that the size of
\langle\mathcal{X},\mathsf{cap}\rangle
is
O({n_{v}}+{n_{e}})
, thereby concluding the proof of Theorem
1.1
.

See
B.1

Proof.

We prove each direction separately.

Proof of correctness(Hamiltonian cycle
\Rightarrow
Consistency).

Given a Hamiltonian cycle in
G
,
assuming it is of form
v_{1}\rightarrow v_{2}\rightarrow\dots\rightarrow v_{{n_{v}}}\rightarrow v_{1}
,
we sketch the concretization
\sigma
as following.
Here
\sigma
can be represented as a sequence of threads.
That is, we execute all events in each thread according to the thread sequence.

\sigma=A_{cycle}\circ A_{1}\circ\dots\circ A_{{n_{v}}}

where
A_{cycle}
is a sequence of threads constructing the cycle
and each
A_{j}
is a sequence of threads executing the encoded events for unselected outgoing edges from
v_{j}
.

A_{cycle}=\tau_{\mathsf{init}}\cdot\tau_{(v_{1},v_{2})}\cdot\tau_{(v_{2},v_{3})}\cdots\tau_{(v_{{n_{v}}-1},v_{{n_{v}}})},\tau_{(v_{{n_{v}}},v_{1})},\tau_{\mathsf{free}}

We assume the outgoing edges from
v_{j}
are
v_{j_{1}},\dots,v_{j_{q}}
, where
q
is the out-degree of
v_{j}
,
and
v_{j_{p}}
is an edge in the Hamiltonian cycle,
then
A_{j}
can be represented as following.

A_{j}=\tau_{v_{j}}\cdot\tau_{(v_{j},v_{j_{1}})}\cdots\tau_{(v_{j},v_{j_{p-1}})}\cdot\tau_{(v_{j},v_{j_{p+1}})}\cdots\tau_{(v_{j},v_{j_{q}})}

Since every send/receive event has the same value,
we mainly discuss the capacity constraints.
In the first stage, the capacity constraints are clearly met.
That is, the in-degree and out-degree of every node should clearly
\geq 1
,
as otherwise
G
has no Hamiltonian cycle.
Therefore, it’s perfectly fine to send once to
\mathtt{ch}^{\prime}_{v}
and
\mathtt{ch}_{v}
in the first phase.
Similarly,
{n_{e}}\geq{n_{v}}
,
as otherwise
G
has no Hamiltonian cycle,
so that it’s also fine to send
{n_{v}}
times to
\mathsf{cnt}
.

In the second stage, we receive once for every
\mathtt{ch}^{\prime}_{v}
,
and only receive
\mathtt{ch}_{u}
if there is a message inside.
We receive exactly
{n_{v}}
times for
\mathsf{cnt}
.
Therefore, the capacity constraints for the second stage is also met.

Lastly,
for the third phase, a channel
\mathtt{ch}^{\prime}_{u}
is sent at most
\mathsf{in}(u)
times,
i.e., once by each
\tau_{v}
for all
(v,u)\in E
.
Moreover,
a channel
\mathtt{ch}_{u}
is sent at most
\mathsf{in}(u)+\mathsf{out}(u)
times,
i.e., once by each
\tau_{v}
for all
(u,v)\in E
,
and once by each
\tau_{w,u}
for all
(w,u)\in E
.
Therefore, the capacity constrains for the third stage is also met.

Proof of correctness(Consistency
\Rightarrow
Hamiltonian cycle).

In this direction, we prove if the input problem is consistent,
then there is a Hamiltonian cycle.
Firstly we argue that
any concretization of this instance will order all events in
\tau_{v_{i}}
after the
\mathtt{\color[rgb]{0,0.4,0}rcv}(\mathsf{\ell})
in
\tau_{\mathsf{free}}
,
because all
\tau_{v_{i}}
starts with
\mathtt{\color[rgb]{0,0.4,0}rcv}(\alpha)
and only
\tau_{\mathsf{free}}
sends to
\alpha
.
Secondly we argue that
the first thread to execute must be
\tau_{\mathsf{init}}
.
This is because all other threads start with receive events on some channels
and all channels have 0 messages at the beginning.
Therefore, so far we can conclude that in any concretization,
the events executed between
\tau_{\mathsf{init}}
and
\tau_{\mathsf{free}}
must be from thread
\tau_{(v_{i},v_{j})}
for some
i,j
,
which encodes edge
v_{i}\rightarrow v_{j}
.

Also, as
\tau_{v_{i}}
,
\tau_{\mathsf{init}},\tau_{\mathsf{free}}
are composed of event blocks protected by
\mathtt{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.8}snd}(\ell)
and
\mathtt{\color[rgb]{0,0.4,0}rcv}(\ell)
,
we claim that none of these blocks can overlap with each other,
because
\mathsf{\ell}
has capacity 1 (see the construction in Figure
6(a)
).
If any two blocks overlap, this means there are two continuous sends to
\mathsf{\ell}
,
which violates the capacity constraints.
Finally, we can conclude that in any concretization
\sigma
of the instance,
it must be of the following form.

\sigma=\sigma_{1}\circ\sigma_{2}
,
where
\sigma_{1}
is a sequence of atomic blocks from
\tau_{\mathsf{init}},\tau_{\mathsf{free}}
or
\tau_{(v_{i},v_{j})}
and
\sigma_{2}
is a sequence of other events not in
\sigma_{1}
.
Moreover
\sigma_{1}
is of the following form

\sigma_{1}=\tau_{\mathsf{init}}\circ\tau_{(1,p_{1})}\circ\tau_{(p_{1},p_{2})},\dots\circ\tau_{(p_{{n_{v}}-2},p_{{n_{v}}-1})}\circ\tau_{(p_{{n_{v}}-1},1)}\circ\tau^{1}_{\mathsf{free}}

where
\tau^{1}_{\mathsf{free}}
is the atomic block in
\tau_{\mathsf{free}}
.
To verify the form of
\sigma_{1}
, we have the following observations.

•

There must be exactly
{n_{v}}
atomic blocks between
\tau_{\mathsf{init}}
and
\tau^{1}_{\mathsf{free}}
, because
\tau_{\mathsf{init}}
sends
{n_{v}}
times on
\mathsf{cnt}
and
\tau^{1}_{\mathsf{free}}
receives
{n_{e}}
times on
\mathsf{cnt}
.
Every thread
\tau_{(v_{i},v_{j})}
receives once from
\mathsf{cnt}
,
so that as
\mathsf{cap}\mathsf{(\mathsf{cnt})}={n_{e}}
,
there must be exactly
{n_{v}}
edge threads between
\tau_{\mathsf{init}}
and
\tau^{1}_{\mathsf{free}}
.
Otherwise, the
{n_{e}}
send events in
\tau^{1}_{\mathsf{free}}
cannot be executed.

•

If two threads between
\tau_{\mathsf{init}}
and
\tau^{1}_{\mathsf{free}}
are next to each other in
\sigma_{1}
,
then they must share a common node.
For example, if
\tau_{(v_{i},v_{j})}
is immediately before
\tau_{(v_{p},v_{q})}
in
\sigma_{1}
, then
j=p
.
To show this,
\tau_{(v_{p},v_{q})}
will receive
\mathtt{ch}_{v_{p}}
once and only those encoded threads for edges that end in
v_{p}
will send once to
\mathtt{ch}_{v_{p}}
.
This observation proves that the threads between
\tau_{\mathsf{init}}
and
\tau^{1}_{\mathsf{free}}
correspond to a walk in the graph.

•

The first thread immediately after
\tau_{\mathsf{free}}
in
\sigma_{1}
must correspond to an edge starting from
v_{1}
,
because
\tau_{(v_{i},v_{j})}
receives once to
\mathtt{ch}_{v_{i}}
and after
\tau_{\mathsf{free}}
being executed,
only
\mathtt{ch}_{v_{i}}
is not empty.
Also, the last thread immediately before
\tau^{1}_{\mathsf{free}}
must correspond to an edge ending in
v_{1}
,
because
\tau^{1}_{\mathsf{free}}
receives once to
\mathtt{ch}_{v_{1}}
and only edge threads ending in
v_{1}
will send
v_{1}
once.
This observation proves that the threads between
\tau_{\mathsf{init}}
and
\tau^{1}_{\mathsf{free}}
correspond to a cycle in the graph
v_{1}\rightarrow v_{p_{1}}\rightarrow v_{p_{2}}\rightarrow\dots\rightarrow v_{p_{{n_{v}}-1}}\rightarrow v_{1}
.

•

Lastly, we show that every node will appear exactly once in the walk except
v_{1}
appearing twice,
as it is both the starting and ending node of the walk.
We send once to every
\mathtt{ch}^{\prime}_{v_{i}}
in
\tau_{\mathsf{init}}
, and every thread
\tau_{(v_{i},v_{j})}
receives
\mathtt{ch}^{\prime}_{v_{j}}
once.
Therefore, we cannot have two edges in the walk that end in the same node
v_{j}
,
as there is only one message in
\mathtt{ch}^{\prime}_{v_{j}}
.

∎

Given the observations above,
it is proved that the walk we found is indeed a Hamiltonian cycle.

B.2.
Hardness with
2
Threads and no Capacity Restrictions

VCh-rf
takes quadratic time when
t=2
and channels have unrestricted capacity or capacity
\leq 1
(as per Theorem
1.7
).
Does this advantage of limiting threads carry over to
VCh
?
We show that this is not the case as
VCh
remains
NP
-hard when
t=2
, even with no capacity restrictions.

Overview.

Our reduction is from positive 1-in-3 SAT, which takes as input a 3CNF formula
\psi
for which every clause contains three distinct positive literals, and the task is to determine whether there is a truth assignment that makes exactly 1-in-3 literals true in each clause.
Given
\psi
, we construct a corresponding
VCh
instance
\langle\mathcal{X},\mathsf{cap}\rangle
, where events in
\mathcal{X}
comprise two phases.
The first phase guesses a truth assignment for the propositional variables of
\psi
,
while the second phase verifies that every clause satisfies the 1-in-3 property.

x_{1}
\dots
x_{{n_{v}}}
C_{1}
\dots
C_{{n_{c}}}

\tau_{\top}

\tau_{\bot}

(a)

Overall scheme

\mathtt{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.8}snd}(\alpha,v_{i}^{3})
\mathtt{\color[rgb]{0,0.4,0}rcv}(\alpha,v_{i}^{4})
\mathtt{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.8}snd}(\ell_{1},v^{1}_{i})
\mathtt{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.8}snd}(\ell_{2},v^{1}_{i})
\mathtt{\color[rgb]{0,0.4,0}rcv}(\ell_{2},v^{1}_{i})
\mathtt{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.8}snd}(C_{k_{1}},\top)
\dots
\mathtt{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.8}snd}(C_{k_{f_{i}}},\top)
\mathtt{\color[rgb]{0,0.4,0}rcv}(\ell_{1},v^{1}_{i})
\mathtt{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.8}snd}(\alpha,v_{i}^{4})
\mathtt{\color[rgb]{0,0.4,0}rcv}(\alpha,v_{i}^{3})
\mathtt{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.8}snd}(\ell_{2},v^{2}_{i})
\mathtt{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.8}snd}(\ell_{1},v^{2}_{i})
\mathtt{\color[rgb]{0,0.4,0}rcv}(\ell_{1},v^{2}_{i})
\mathtt{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.8}snd}(C_{k_{1}},\bot)
\dots
\mathtt{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.8}snd}(C_{k_{f_{i}}},\bot)
\mathtt{\color[rgb]{0,0.4,0}rcv}(\ell_{2},v^{2}_{i})
A^{\top}_{i}
A^{\bot}_{i}

\tau_{\top}

\tau_{\bot}

(b)

Events for proposition
x_{i}

\mathtt{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.8}snd}(\alpha,w_{j}^{4})
\mathtt{\color[rgb]{0,0.4,0}rcv}(\alpha,w_{j}^{5})
\mathtt{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.8}snd}(\ell_{1},w^{1}_{j})
\mathtt{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.8}snd}(\ell_{2},w^{1}_{j})
\mathtt{\color[rgb]{0,0.4,0}rcv}(\ell_{2},w^{1}_{j})
\mathtt{\color[rgb]{0,0.4,0}rcv}(C_{j},\top)
\mathtt{\color[rgb]{0,0.4,0}rcv}(C_{j},\bot)
\mathtt{\color[rgb]{0,0.4,0}rcv}(\ell_{1},w^{1}_{j})
\mathtt{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.8}snd}(\alpha,w_{j}^{5})
\mathtt{\color[rgb]{0,0.4,0}rcv}(\alpha,w_{j}^{4})
\mathtt{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.8}snd}(\ell_{2},w^{2}_{j})
\mathtt{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.8}snd}(\ell_{1},w^{2}_{j})
\mathtt{\color[rgb]{0,0.4,0}rcv}(\ell_{1},w^{2}_{j})
\mathtt{\color[rgb]{0,0.4,0}rcv}(C_{j},\bot)
\mathtt{\color[rgb]{0,0.4,0}rcv}(C_{j},\top)
\mathtt{\color[rgb]{0,0.4,0}rcv}(\ell_{2},w^{2}_{j})
\mathtt{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.8}snd}(\ell_{2},w^{3}_{j})
\mathtt{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.8}snd}(\ell_{1},w^{3}_{j})
\mathtt{\color[rgb]{0,0.4,0}rcv}(\ell_{1},w^{3}_{j})
\mathtt{\color[rgb]{0,0.4,0}rcv}(C_{j},\bot)
\mathtt{\color[rgb]{0,0.4,0}rcv}(C_{j},\top)
\mathtt{\color[rgb]{0,0.4,0}rcv}(\ell_{2},w^{3}_{j})
B^{\top}_{j}
B^{\bot}_{j}

\tau_{\top}

\tau_{\bot}

(c)

Events for clause
C_{j}
(
B^{\top}_{j},B^{\bot}_{j}
)

.

Reduction from positive 1-in-3 SAT to
VCh
with
2

(
15(b)
) shows the encoding events for
A^{\top}_{i},A^{\bot}_{i}
,

Reduction.

Given a formula
\psi
with
{n_{v}}
propositional variables and
{n_{c}}
clauses,
we construct a
VCh
instance
\langle\mathcal{X},\mathsf{cap}\rangle
where
\mathcal{X}
comprises
2
threads
\tau_{\top}
and
\tau_{\bot}
,
and
{n_{c}}+3
capacity-unbounded channels

\{\ell_{1},\ell_{2},\alpha,C_{1},\dots,C_{{n_{c}}}\}
.
Figure
15
illustrates the overall scheme.
For each
p\in\{\top,\bot\}
, the thread
\tau_{p}
consists of two sequential phases,
corresponding to propositional variables and clauses on
\psi
.

\displaystyle\tau_{p}=A^{p}_{1}\cdots A^{p}_{{n_{v}}}\cdot B^{p}_{1}\cdots B^{p}_{{n_{c}}}

The events in
A^{p}_{i}
correspond to the
i^{\text{th}}
variable
x_{i}
,
while the events in
B^{p}_{j}
correspond to the
j^{\text{th}}
clause
C_{j}
.
Let
C_{k_{1}},C_{k_{2}},\ldots,C_{k_{f_{i}}}
be an ordered list of clauses in which variable
x_{i}
appears.
The above sequences make use of the atomicity gadget (Figure
6(b)
), and are defined as follows.

\displaystyle\begin{array}[]{rcl}A^{\top}_{i}&=&\!\!\!\!\!\begin{aligned} \begin{array}[]{l}\;\mathtt{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.8}snd}(\alpha,v_{i}^{3})\cdot\mathtt{\color[rgb]{0,0.4,0}rcv}(\alpha,v_{i}^{4})\cdot\mathtt{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.8}snd}(\ell_{1},v^{1}_{i})\cdot\mathtt{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.8}snd}(\ell_{2},v^{1}_{i}))\cdot\mathtt{\color[rgb]{0,0.4,0}rcv}(\ell_{2},v^{1}_{i}))\\
\cdot\mathtt{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.8}snd}(C_{k_{1}},\top)\cdots\mathtt{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.8}snd}(C_{k_{f_{i}}},\top)\cdot\mathtt{\color[rgb]{0,0.4,0}rcv}(\ell_{1},v^{1}_{i})\end{array}\end{aligned}\\
\vskip 2.84544ptA^{\bot}_{i}&=&\!\!\!\!\!\begin{aligned} \begin{array}[]{l}\;\mathtt{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.8}snd}(\alpha,v_{i}^{4})\cdot\mathtt{\color[rgb]{0,0.4,0}rcv}(\alpha,v_{i}^{3})\cdot\mathtt{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.8}snd}(\ell_{2},v^{2}_{i})\cdot\mathtt{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.8}snd}(\ell_{1},v^{2}_{i})\cdot\mathtt{\color[rgb]{0,0.4,0}rcv}(\ell_{1},v^{2}_{i})\\
\cdot\mathtt{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.8}snd}(C_{k_{1}},\bot)\cdots\mathtt{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.8}snd}(C_{k_{f_{i}}},\bot)\cdot\mathtt{\color[rgb]{0,0.4,0}rcv}(\ell_{2},v^{2}_{i})\end{array}\end{aligned}\\
\vskip 2.84544ptB^{\top}_{j}&=&\!\!\!\!\!\begin{aligned} \begin{array}[]{l}\;\mathtt{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.8}snd}(\alpha,w^{4}_{j})\cdot\mathtt{\color[rgb]{0,0.4,0}rcv}(\alpha,w^{5}_{j})\cdot\mathtt{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.8}snd}(\ell_{1},w^{1}_{j})\cdot\mathtt{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.8}snd}(\ell_{2},w^{1}_{j})\cdot\mathtt{\color[rgb]{0,0.4,0}rcv}(\ell_{2},w^{1}_{j})\\
\cdot\mathtt{\color[rgb]{0,0.4,0}rcv}(C_{j},\top)\cdot\mathtt{\color[rgb]{0,0.4,0}rcv}(C_{j},\bot)\cdot\mathtt{\color[rgb]{0,0.4,0}rcv}(\ell_{1},w^{1}_{j})\end{array}\end{aligned}\\
\vskip 2.84544ptB^{\bot}_{j}&=&\!\!\!\!\!\begin{aligned} \begin{array}[]{l}\;\mathtt{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.8}snd}(\alpha,w^{5}_{j})\cdot\mathtt{\color[rgb]{0,0.4,0}rcv}(\alpha,w^{4}_{j})\cdot\mathtt{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.8}snd}(\ell_{2},w^{2}_{j})\cdot\mathtt{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.8}snd}(\ell_{1},w^{2}_{j}))\cdot\mathtt{\color[rgb]{0,0.4,0}rcv}(\ell_{1},w^{2}_{j}))\cdot\mathtt{\color[rgb]{0,0.4,0}rcv}(C_{j},\bot)\cdot\mathtt{\color[rgb]{0,0.4,0}rcv}(C_{j},\top)\\
\cdot\mathtt{\color[rgb]{0,0.4,0}rcv}(\ell_{2},w^{2}_{j}))\cdot\mathtt{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.8}snd}(\ell_{2},w^{3}_{j}))\cdot\mathtt{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.8}snd}(\ell_{1},w^{3}_{j})\cdot\mathtt{\color[rgb]{0,0.4,0}rcv}(\ell_{1},w^{3}_{j})\cdot\mathtt{\color[rgb]{0,0.4,0}rcv}(C_{j},\bot)\cdot\mathtt{\color[rgb]{0,0.4,0}rcv}(C_{j},\top)\cdot\mathtt{\color[rgb]{0,0.4,0}rcv}(\ell_{2},w^{3}_{j})\end{array}\end{aligned}\end{array}

where
v^{1}_{i},v^{2}_{i},v^{3}_{i},v^{4}_{i}
and
w^{1}_{j},w^{2}_{j},w^{3}_{j},w^{4}_{j},w^{5}_{j}
are distinct values associated with
i
and
j
,
guaranteeing atomicity and ensuring that the encoded events for different variables or clauses appear sequentially.

Lemma B.2.

\langle\mathcal{X},\mathsf{cap}\rangle
is consistent iff
\psi
is 1-in-3 satisfiable.

Finally, our reduction takes
O({n_{v}}+{n_{c}})
time, thereby concluding Theorem
1.2
.

See
B.2

Proof.

We prove each direction separately.

Correctness (Satisfiability
\Rightarrow
Consistency).

Given an assignment that satisfies
\psi
,
we encode a concretization
\sigma
of
\langle\mathcal{X},\mathsf{cap}\rangle
as following.

\sigma=A_{1}\cdots A_{n}\cdot B_{1}\cdots B_{m}

where
A_{i}
is an interleaving of
A^{\top}_{i},A^{\bot}_{i}

and
B_{j}
is an interleaving of
B^{\top}_{j},B^{\bot}_{j}
.
Moreover, if
x_{i}
is assigned to be true,
then

\displaystyle A_{i}

\displaystyle=\mathtt{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.8}snd}(\alpha,v_{i}^{3})\cdot\mathtt{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.8}snd}(\alpha,v_{i}^{4})\cdot\mathtt{\color[rgb]{0,0.4,0}rcv}(\alpha,v_{i}^{3})\cdot\mathtt{\color[rgb]{0,0.4,0}rcv}(\alpha,v_{i}^{4})

\displaystyle\cdot\mathtt{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.8}snd}(\ell_{1},v_{i}^{1})\cdot\mathtt{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.8}snd}(\ell_{2},v_{i}^{1})\cdot\mathtt{\color[rgb]{0,0.4,0}rcv}(\ell_{2},v_{i}^{1})\cdot\mathtt{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.8}snd}(C_{i,1},{\top})\cdots\mathtt{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.8}snd}(C_{i,k_{i}},{\top})\cdot\mathtt{\color[rgb]{0,0.4,0}rcv}(\ell_{1},v_{i}^{1})\;(\mathsf{from}\;\tau_{\top})

\displaystyle\cdot\mathtt{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.8}snd}(\ell_{2},v_{i}^{2})\cdot\mathtt{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.8}snd}(\ell_{1},v_{i}^{2})\cdot\mathtt{\color[rgb]{0,0.4,0}rcv}(\ell_{1},v_{i}^{2})\cdot\mathtt{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.8}snd}(C_{i,1},{\bot})\cdots\mathtt{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.8}snd}(C_{i,k_{i}},{\bot})\cdot\mathtt{\color[rgb]{0,0.4,0}rcv}(\ell_{2},v_{i}^{2})\;(\mathsf{from}\;\tau_{\bot})

Otherwise if
x_{i}
is assigned to be false,
then

\displaystyle A_{i}

\displaystyle=\mathtt{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.8}snd}(\alpha,v_{i}^{3})\cdot\mathtt{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.8}snd}(\alpha,v_{i}^{4})\cdot\mathtt{\color[rgb]{0,0.4,0}rcv}(\alpha,v_{i}^{3})\cdot\mathtt{\color[rgb]{0,0.4,0}rcv}(\alpha,v_{i}^{4})

\displaystyle\cdot\mathtt{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.8}snd}(\ell_{2},v_{i}^{2})\cdot\mathtt{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.8}snd}(\ell_{1},v_{i}^{2})\cdot\mathtt{\color[rgb]{0,0.4,0}rcv}(\ell_{1},v_{i}^{2})\cdot\mathtt{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.8}snd}(C_{i,1},{\bot})\cdots\mathtt{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.8}snd}(C_{i,k_{i}},{\bot})\cdot\mathtt{\color[rgb]{0,0.4,0}rcv}(\ell_{2},v_{i}^{2})\;(\mathsf{from}\;\tau_{\bot})

\displaystyle\cdot\mathtt{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.8}snd}(\ell_{1},v_{i}^{1})\cdot\mathtt{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.8}snd}(\ell_{2},v_{i}^{1})\cdot\mathtt{\color[rgb]{0,0.4,0}rcv}(\ell_{2},v_{i}^{1})\cdot\mathtt{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.8}snd}(C_{i,1},{\top})\cdots\mathtt{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.8}snd}(C_{i,k_{i}},{\top})\cdot\mathtt{\color[rgb]{0,0.4,0}rcv}(\ell_{1},v_{i}^{1})\;(\mathsf{from}\;\tau_{\top})

For
B_{j}
, we sort the variables in clause
C_{j}
by the variable index.
Then there are three possibilities,
i.e., the variable assigned to be true can be the first, second or third variable in
C_{j}
.
If it is the first one, then

\displaystyle B_{j}

\displaystyle=\mathtt{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.8}snd}(\alpha,w_{j}^{4})\cdot\mathtt{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.8}snd}(\alpha,w_{j}^{5})\cdot\mathtt{\color[rgb]{0,0.4,0}rcv}(\alpha,w_{j}^{4})\cdot\mathtt{\color[rgb]{0,0.4,0}rcv}(\alpha,w_{j}^{5})

\displaystyle\cdot\mathtt{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.8}snd}(\ell_{1},w_{j}^{1})\cdot\mathtt{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.8}snd}(\ell_{2},w_{j}^{1})\cdot\mathtt{\color[rgb]{0,0.4,0}rcv}(\ell_{2},w_{j}^{1})\cdot\mathtt{\color[rgb]{0,0.4,0}rcv}(C_{j},{\top})\cdot\mathtt{\color[rgb]{0,0.4,0}rcv}(C_{j},{\bot})\cdot\mathtt{\color[rgb]{0,0.4,0}rcv}(\ell_{1},w_{j}^{1})\;(\mathsf{from}\;\tau_{\top})

\displaystyle\cdot\mathtt{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.8}snd}(\ell_{2},w_{j}^{2})\cdot\mathtt{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.8}snd}(\ell_{1},w_{j}^{2})\cdot\mathtt{\color[rgb]{0,0.4,0}rcv}(\ell_{1},w_{j}^{2})\cdot\mathtt{\color[rgb]{0,0.4,0}rcv}(C_{j},{\bot})\cdot\mathtt{\color[rgb]{0,0.4,0}rcv}(C_{j},{\top})\cdot\mathtt{\color[rgb]{0,0.4,0}rcv}(\ell_{2},w_{j}^{2})\;(\mathsf{from}\;\tau_{\bot})

\displaystyle\cdot\mathtt{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.8}snd}(\ell_{2},w_{j}^{3})\cdot\mathtt{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.8}snd}(\ell_{1},w_{j}^{3})\cdot\mathtt{\color[rgb]{0,0.4,0}rcv}(\ell_{1},w_{j}^{3})\cdot\mathtt{\color[rgb]{0,0.4,0}rcv}(C_{j},{\bot})\cdot\mathtt{\color[rgb]{0,0.4,0}rcv}(C_{j},{\top})\cdot\mathtt{\color[rgb]{0,0.4,0}rcv}(\ell_{2},w_{j}^{3})\;(\mathsf{from}\;\tau_{\bot})

If it is the second one, then

\displaystyle B_{j}

\displaystyle=\mathtt{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.8}snd}(\alpha,w_{j}^{4})\cdot\mathtt{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.8}snd}(\alpha,w_{j}^{5})\cdot\mathtt{\color[rgb]{0,0.4,0}rcv}(\alpha,w_{j}^{4})\cdot\mathtt{\color[rgb]{0,0.4,0}rcv}(\alpha,w_{j}^{5})

\displaystyle\cdot\mathtt{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.8}snd}(\ell_{2},w_{j}^{2})\cdot\mathtt{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.8}snd}(\ell_{1},w_{j}^{2})\cdot\mathtt{\color[rgb]{0,0.4,0}rcv}(\ell_{1},w_{j}^{2})\cdot\mathtt{\color[rgb]{0,0.4,0}rcv}(C_{j},{\bot})\cdot\mathtt{\color[rgb]{0,0.4,0}rcv}(C_{j},{\top})\cdot\mathtt{\color[rgb]{0,0.4,0}rcv}(\ell_{2},w_{j}^{2})\;(\mathsf{from}\;\tau_{\bot})

\displaystyle\cdot\mathtt{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.8}snd}(\ell_{1},w_{j}^{1})\cdot\mathtt{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.8}snd}(\ell_{2},w_{j}^{1})\cdot\mathtt{\color[rgb]{0,0.4,0}rcv}(\ell_{2},w_{j}^{1})\cdot\mathtt{\color[rgb]{0,0.4,0}rcv}(C_{j},{\top})\cdot\mathtt{\color[rgb]{0,0.4,0}rcv}(C_{j},{\bot})\cdot\mathtt{\color[rgb]{0,0.4,0}rcv}(\ell_{1},w_{j}^{1})\;(\mathsf{from}\;\tau_{\top})

\displaystyle\cdot\mathtt{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.8}snd}(\ell_{2},w_{j}^{3})\cdot\mathtt{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.8}snd}(\ell_{1},w_{j}^{3})\cdot\mathtt{\color[rgb]{0,0.4,0}rcv}(\ell_{1},w_{j}^{3})\cdot\mathtt{\color[rgb]{0,0.4,0}rcv}(C_{j},{\bot})\cdot\mathtt{\color[rgb]{0,0.4,0}rcv}(C_{j},{\top})\cdot\mathtt{\color[rgb]{0,0.4,0}rcv}(\ell_{2},w_{j}^{3})\;(\mathsf{from}\;\tau_{\bot})

If it is the third one, then

\displaystyle B_{j}

\displaystyle=\mathtt{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.8}snd}(\alpha,w_{j}^{4})\cdot\mathtt{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.8}snd}(\alpha,w_{j}^{5})\cdot\mathtt{\color[rgb]{0,0.4,0}rcv}(\alpha,w_{j}^{4})\cdot\mathtt{\color[rgb]{0,0.4,0}rcv}(\alpha,w_{j}^{5})

\displaystyle\cdot\mathtt{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.8}snd}(\ell_{2},w_{j}^{2})\cdot\mathtt{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.8}snd}(\ell_{1},w_{j}^{2})\cdot\mathtt{\color[rgb]{0,0.4,0}rcv}(\ell_{1},w_{j}^{2})\cdot\mathtt{\color[rgb]{0,0.4,0}rcv}(C_{j},{\bot})\cdot\mathtt{\color[rgb]{0,0.4,0}rcv}(C_{j},{\top})\cdot\mathtt{\color[rgb]{0,0.4,0}rcv}(\ell_{2},w_{j}^{2})\;(\mathsf{from}\;\tau_{\bot})

\displaystyle\cdot\mathtt{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.8}snd}(\ell_{2},w_{j}^{3})\cdot\mathtt{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.8}snd}(\ell_{1},w_{j}^{3})\cdot\mathtt{\color[rgb]{0,0.4,0}rcv}(\ell_{1},w_{j}^{3})\cdot\mathtt{\color[rgb]{0,0.4,0}rcv}(C_{j},{\bot})\cdot\mathtt{\color[rgb]{0,0.4,0}rcv}(C_{j},{\top})\cdot\mathtt{\color[rgb]{0,0.4,0}rcv}(\ell_{2},w_{j}^{3})\;(\mathsf{from}\;\tau_{\bot})

\displaystyle\cdot\mathtt{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.8}snd}(\ell_{1},w_{j}^{1})\cdot\mathtt{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.8}snd}(\ell_{2},w_{j}^{1})\cdot\mathtt{\color[rgb]{0,0.4,0}rcv}(\ell_{2},w_{j}^{1})\cdot\mathtt{\color[rgb]{0,0.4,0}rcv}(C_{j},{\top})\cdot\mathtt{\color[rgb]{0,0.4,0}rcv}(C_{j},{\bot})\cdot\mathtt{\color[rgb]{0,0.4,0}rcv}(\ell_{1},w_{j}^{1})\;(\mathsf{from}\;\tau_{\top})

The
po
and capacity constraints are clearly satisfied.
We now argue the value constraints are also satisfied.
For
\alpha,\ell_{1},\ell_{2}
, the value constraints are satisfied in each
A_{i},B_{j}
.
Now for each
C_{j}
, we consider the value sent to this channel,
and claim it is one of the three situations,
i.e.,
[
\top,\bot,\bot,\top,\bot,\top
] (first literal in
C_{j}
is true),
[
\bot,\top,\top,\bot,\bot,\top
](second literal in
C_{j}
is true),
[
\bot,\top,\bot,\top,\top,\bot
](third literal in
C_{j}
is true).
This is because every clause has distinct variables and we schedule
A_{i}
sequentially.
This value pattern is exactly matched by
B_{j}
.

Correctness (Consistency
\Rightarrow
Satisfiability).

Now we show if
\langle\mathcal{X},\mathsf{cap}\rangle
is consistent,
then
\psi
is 1-in-3 satisfiable.
Given a concretization
\sigma
of
\langle\mathcal{X},\mathsf{cap}\rangle
,
we assign values for each
x_{i}
as following.
Since
A^{\top}_{i},A^{\bot}_{i}
both contain send events to
C_{i,1},\dots,C_{i,k_{i}}
,
which are protected by channel
\ell_{1}
and
\ell_{2}
,
these send events should be executed atomically (see Figure
6(b)
for explanation).
That is, for encoded events of each variable
x_{i}
,
either all send events with value
\top
are before all send events with value
\bot
or
all send events with value
\bot
are before all send events with value
\top
.
We assign
x_{i}
to be true iff in
\sigma
,
all send events with value
\top
are before all send events with value
\bot
.

Now we prove that this assignment satisfies that in
\psi
,
there is one and only one variable in an arbitrary clause
C_{j}
being assigned true.
There are totally six messages being sent to each channel
C_{j}
.
That is, encoded events for each variable in clause
C_{j}
will send two messages to channel
C_{j}

and there are three variables in clause
C_{j}
.
We consider the value sending to channel
C_{j}
,
and can observe the
k
-th and
k+1
-th value are either
[\bot,{\top}]
or
[\top,{\bot}]
for all
k=1,3,5
,
because a variable will send both
\top,\bot
once and
C_{j}
has no duplicated variables.
If
x_{i}
is assigned to be true, then it corresponds to a message sequence of
[\top,{\bot}]
,
and otherwise if
x_{i}
is assigned to be false, then it corresponds to a message sequence of
[\bot,{\top}]
.
In
B^{\top}_{j},B^{\bot}_{j}
,
we require in the three message sequences,
exactly one of them should be
[\top,{\bot}]
and the other two should be
[\bot,{\top}]
,
which guarantees exactly one of the three variables in clause
C_{j}
is assigned to be true.
Therefore,
\psi
is 1-in-3 satisfiable.
∎

B.3.
Hardness with
1
Channel

Finally, in this section we prove the hardness of
VCh
even when threads communicate over a single channel, which can be either synchronous or have capacity
1
(as per Theorem
1.3
).

Overview.

Our reduction is from positive 1-in-3 SAT.
Given
\psi
, we construct a corresponding
VCh
instance
\langle\mathcal{X},\mathsf{cap}\rangle
with only one channel (either synchronous, or asynchronous with capacity
1
), where events in
\mathcal{X}
comprise two phases.
The first phase guesses an assignment of the propositional variables of
\psi
,
while the second phase verifies that every clause satisfies the 1-in-3 property, and executes residual events from the first phase.
The construction works for both when the unique channel is synchronous and when it has capacity
1
).

\mathtt{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.8}snd}(x_{1})
\dots
\mathtt{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.8}snd}(x_{{n_{v}}})
\mathtt{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.8}snd}(\bar{x}_{1})
\dots
\mathtt{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.8}snd}(\bar{x}_{{n_{v}}})
\mathtt{\color[rgb]{0,0.4,0}rcv}_{1}(\gamma)
\dots
\mathtt{\color[rgb]{0,0.4,0}rcv}_{{n_{v}}}(\gamma)
\mathtt{\color[rgb]{0,0.4,0}rcv}_{1}(\beta)
\dots
\mathtt{\color[rgb]{0,0.4,0}rcv}_{n_{c}}(\beta)
\mathtt{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.8}snd}_{1}(\alpha)
\dots
\mathtt{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.8}snd}_{2{{n_{v}}}+{n_{c}}+1}(\alpha)
\mathtt{\color[rgb]{0,0.4,0}rcv}_{2{{n_{v}}}+{n_{c}}+1}(\alpha)
\mathtt{\color[rgb]{0,0.4,0}rcv}_{1}(\gamma)
\dots
\mathtt{\color[rgb]{0,0.4,0}rcv}_{{n_{v}}}(\gamma)

\tau_{\top}

\tau_{\bot}

\tau_{\sf conn}

\tau_{\gamma}

(a)

Auxiliary threads

\mathtt{\color[rgb]{0,0.4,0}rcv}(x_{i})
\mathtt{\color[rgb]{0,0.4,0}rcv}(\bar{x}_{i})
\mathtt{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.8}snd}(C_{i,1})
\dots
\mathtt{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.8}snd}(C_{i,f_{i}})
\mathtt{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.8}snd}(\gamma)
\mathtt{\color[rgb]{0,0.4,0}rcv}(\bar{x}_{i})
\mathtt{\color[rgb]{0,0.4,0}rcv}(x_{i})
\mathtt{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.8}snd}(\gamma)
\mathtt{\color[rgb]{0,0.4,0}rcv}_{2i-1}(\alpha)
\mathtt{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.8}snd}(x_{i})
\mathtt{\color[rgb]{0,0.4,0}rcv}_{2i}(\alpha)
\mathtt{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.8}snd}(\bar{x}_{i})

\tau_{i,1}

\tau_{i,2}

\tau_{i,3}

\tau_{i,4}

(b)

Threads for variable
x_{i}

\mathtt{\color[rgb]{0,0.4,0}rcv}(C_{j})
\mathtt{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.8}snd}(\beta)
\mathtt{\color[rgb]{0,0.4,0}rcv}_{2n+j}(\alpha)
\mathtt{\color[rgb]{0,0.4,0}rcv}(C_{j})
\mathtt{\color[rgb]{0,0.4,0}rcv}(C_{j})

\tau_{j}^{1}

\tau_{j}^{2}

(c)

Threads for clause
C_{j}

Figure 16
.
An example of the reduction from positive one-in-three satisfiability.

Reduction.

Figure
16
illustrates this construction.
Given a formula
\psi
with
{n_{v}}
variables and
{n_{c}}
clauses,

\langle\mathcal{X},\mathsf{cap}\rangle
has
4+4{n_{v}}+2{n_{c}}
threads

\{\tau_{\top},\tau_{\bot},\tau_{\gamma},\tau_{\sf conn}\}

\uplus\{\tau_{i,1},\tau_{i,2},\tau_{i,3},\tau_{i,4}\,|\,1\leq i\leq{n_{v}}\}

\uplus\{\tau_{j}^{1},\tau_{j}^{2}\,|\,1\leq j\leq{n_{c}}\}
.
Since we use a single channel
\mathtt{ch}
, we will omit explicitly mention it
and use the shorthand
\mathtt{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.8}snd}(\mathsf{val})
or
\mathtt{\color[rgb]{0,0.4,0}rcv}(\mathsf{val})
to denote
send and receive events on
\mathtt{ch}
with value
\mathsf{val}
.
We first describe the sequences of the
4
auxiliary threads:

\displaystyle\begin{array}[]{l}\tau_{\top}=\mathtt{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.8}snd}(x_{1})\cdots\mathtt{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.8}snd}(x_{{n_{v}}})\quad\tau_{\bot}=\mathtt{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.8}snd}(\bar{x}_{1})\cdots\mathtt{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.8}snd}(\bar{x}_{{n_{v}}})\quad\tau_{\gamma}=\mathtt{\color[rgb]{0,0.4,0}rcv}_{2{n_{v}}+{n_{c}}+1}(\alpha)\cdot\mathtt{\color[rgb]{0,0.4,0}rcv}_{1}(\gamma)\cdots\mathtt{\color[rgb]{0,0.4,0}rcv}_{{n_{v}}}(\gamma)\\
\tau_{\sf conn}=\mathtt{\color[rgb]{0,0.4,0}rcv}_{1}(\gamma)\cdots\mathtt{\color[rgb]{0,0.4,0}rcv}_{{n_{v}}}(\gamma)\cdot\mathtt{\color[rgb]{0,0.4,0}rcv}_{1}(\beta)\cdots\mathtt{\color[rgb]{0,0.4,0}rcv}_{{n_{c}}}(\beta)\cdot\mathtt{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.8}snd}_{1}(\alpha)\cdots\mathtt{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.8}snd}_{2{n_{v}}+{n_{c}}+1}(\alpha)\\
\end{array}

Here,
\alpha,\beta,\gamma,x_{1},\ldots,x_{n_{1}},\bar{x}_{1},\ldots,\bar{x}_{{n_{v}}}

are distinct values.
Next, we describe the content of thread the four threads corresponding to each variable
x_{i}
in
\psi
:

\displaystyle\begin{array}[]{rclcrcl}\tau_{i,1}&=&\mathtt{\color[rgb]{0,0.4,0}rcv}(x_{i})\cdot\mathtt{\color[rgb]{0,0.4,0}rcv}(\bar{x}_{i})\cdot\mathtt{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.8}snd}(C_{i,1})\cdots\mathtt{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.8}snd}(C_{i,f_{i}})\cdot\mathtt{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.8}snd}(\gamma)&&\tau_{i,2}&=&\mathtt{\color[rgb]{0,0.4,0}rcv}(\bar{x}_{i})\cdot\mathtt{\color[rgb]{0,0.4,0}rcv}(x_{i})\cdot\mathtt{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.8}snd}(\gamma)\\
\tau_{i,3}&=&\mathtt{\color[rgb]{0,0.4,0}rcv}_{2i-1}(\alpha)\cdot\mathtt{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.8}snd}(x_{i})&&\tau_{i,4}&=&\mathtt{\color[rgb]{0,0.4,0}rcv}(\alpha)_{2i}\cdot\mathtt{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.8}snd}(\bar{x}_{i})\end{array}

where
f_{i}
is the frequency of
x_{i}
in
\psi
,
and
C_{i,p}
is the clause in which
x_{i}
appears for the
p^{\text{th}}
time.
Finally, we have two threads for each clause
C_{j}
:

\displaystyle\begin{array}[]{ccc}\tau_{j}^{1}=\mathtt{\color[rgb]{0,0.4,0}rcv}(C_{j})\cdot\mathtt{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.8}snd}(\beta)&&\tau_{j}^{2}=\mathtt{\color[rgb]{0,0.4,0}rcv}_{2{n_{v}}+j}(\alpha)\cdot\mathtt{\color[rgb]{0,0.4,0}rcv}(C_{j})\cdot\mathtt{\color[rgb]{0,0.4,0}rcv}(C_{j})\end{array}

The following lemma states the correctness of the construction.

Lemma B.3.

\langle\mathcal{X},\mathsf{cap}\rangle
is consistent iff
\psi
is 1-in-3 satisfiable.

Overall,
\langle\mathcal{X},\mathsf{cap}\rangle
has
O({n_{v}}+{n_{c}})
events,
concluding Theorem
1.3
.

See
B.3

Proof.

We prove each direction separately.

Proof of correctness (Satisfiability
\Rightarrow
Consistency).

Given an assignment that satisfies
\psi
, we sketch the concretization
\sigma
as following.
In general
\sigma=\sigma_{1}\circ\sigma_{2}
,
where
\sigma_{1}
encodes the execution of
\tau_{\top},\tau_{\bot},\tau_{j_{1}}
for all
1\leq j\leq m
and one of
\tau_{i,1},\tau_{i,2}
for each
1\leq i\leq n
,
and
\sigma_{2}
is a sequence of the rest events.

\sigma_{1}=S_{1}\circ\dots\circ S_{{n_{v}}}

where
S_{i}
is a sequence of events we encode for variable
x_{i}
.
For convenience, when multiple threads contain send or receive events with the same value, then we denote events as
\mathtt{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.8}snd}(a,t)
to show that this is an event
\mathtt{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.8}snd}(a)
from thread
t
.
If
x_{i}
is assigned to be true,
let clauses
C_{i,1},\dots,C_{i,f_{i}}
be all the clauses
x_{i}
appears in.

\displaystyle S_{i}

\displaystyle=\mathtt{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.8}snd}(x_{i},\tau_{\top})\cdot\mathtt{\color[rgb]{0,0.4,0}rcv}(x_{i},\tau_{i,1})\cdot\mathtt{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.8}snd}(\bar{x}_{i},\tau_{\bot})\cdot\mathtt{\color[rgb]{0,0.4,0}rcv}(\bar{x}_{i},\tau_{i,1})

\displaystyle\cdot\mathtt{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.8}snd}(C_{i,1},\tau_{i,1})\cdot\mathtt{\color[rgb]{0,0.4,0}rcv}(C_{i,1},\tau_{i_{1}}^{1})\cdots\mathtt{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.8}snd}(C_{i,f_{i}},\tau_{i,1})\cdot\mathtt{\color[rgb]{0,0.4,0}rcv}(C_{i,f_{i}},\tau_{f_{i}}^{1})\cdot\mathtt{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.8}snd}(\gamma,\tau_{i,1})\cdot\mathtt{\color[rgb]{0,0.4,0}rcv}(\gamma,\tau_{conn})

Otherwise

S_{i}=\mathtt{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.8}snd}(\bar{x}_{i},\tau_{\bot})\cdot\mathtt{\color[rgb]{0,0.4,0}rcv}(\bar{x}_{i},\tau_{i,2})\cdot\mathtt{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.8}snd}(x_{i},\tau_{\top})\cdot\mathtt{\color[rgb]{0,0.4,0}rcv}(x_{i},\tau_{i,2})\cdot\mathtt{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.8}snd}(\gamma,\tau_{i,2})\cdot\mathtt{\color[rgb]{0,0.4,0}rcv}(\gamma,\tau_{conn})

Now we describe the details of
\sigma_{2}=B_{0}\circ B_{1}\circ\dots\circ B_{n}
,
where
B_{0}
is a sequence of events to link two phases and
B_{i}
(
i>0
) is the encoded events for
x_{i}
.

\displaystyle B_{0}

\displaystyle=\mathtt{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.8}snd}(\beta,\tau^{1}_{1})\cdot\mathtt{\color[rgb]{0,0.4,0}rcv}_{1}(\beta)\cdots\mathtt{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.8}snd}(\beta,\tau^{1}_{{n_{c}}})\cdot\mathtt{\color[rgb]{0,0.4,0}rcv}_{n_{c}}(\beta)

\displaystyle\cdot\mathtt{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.8}snd}_{1}(\alpha)\cdot\mathtt{\color[rgb]{0,0.4,0}rcv}_{1}(\alpha)\cdots\mathtt{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.8}snd}_{2{n_{v}}+{n_{c}}+1}(\alpha)\cdot\mathtt{\color[rgb]{0,0.4,0}rcv}_{2{n_{v}}+{n_{c}}+1}(\alpha)

Here
\mathtt{\color[rgb]{0,0.4,0}rcv}(\alpha)
are just the first event in every
\tau_{i,3},\tau_{i,4},\tau_{j}^{2},\tau_{\gamma}
.
Any permutations of these events suffice.

If
x_{i}
is assigned to be true, then

\displaystyle B_{i}

\displaystyle=\mathtt{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.8}snd}(\bar{x}_{i},\tau_{i,4})\cdot\mathtt{\color[rgb]{0,0.4,0}rcv}(\bar{x}_{i},\tau_{i,2})\cdot\mathtt{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.8}snd}(x_{i},\tau_{i,3})\cdot\mathtt{\color[rgb]{0,0.4,0}rcv}(x_{i},\tau_{i,2})\cdot\mathtt{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.8}snd}(\gamma,\tau_{i,2})\cdot\mathtt{\color[rgb]{0,0.4,0}rcv}(\gamma,\tau_{\gamma})

Otherwise,

\displaystyle B_{i}

\displaystyle=\mathtt{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.8}snd}(x_{i},\tau_{i,3})\cdot\mathtt{\color[rgb]{0,0.4,0}rcv}(x_{i},\tau_{i,1})\cdot\mathtt{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.8}snd}(\bar{x}_{i},\tau_{i,4})\cdot\mathtt{\color[rgb]{0,0.4,0}rcv}(\bar{x}_{i},\tau_{i,1})\cdot\mathtt{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.8}snd}(C_{i,1},\tau_{i,1})\cdot\mathtt{\color[rgb]{0,0.4,0}rcv}(C_{i,1},\tau_{i_{1}}^{2})

\displaystyle\cdot\mathtt{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.8}snd}(C_{i,2},\tau_{i,1})\cdot\mathtt{\color[rgb]{0,0.4,0}rcv}(C_{i,2},\tau_{i_{2}}^{2})\cdot\mathtt{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.8}snd}(C_{i,3},\tau_{i,1})\cdot\mathtt{\color[rgb]{0,0.4,0}rcv}(C_{i,3},\tau_{i_{3}}^{2}),\mathtt{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.8}snd}(\gamma,\tau_{i,1})\cdot\mathtt{\color[rgb]{0,0.4,0}rcv}(\gamma,\tau_{\gamma})

The program order is clearly satisfied.
For value constraints, we can observe that in
\sigma
,
a send event is immediately followed by a receive event with the same value,
so that
\sigma
must satisfy value constraints.
Moreover, we note the above completion is also valid when the channel is synchronous,
because a send event is immediately followed by a receive event with the same value from another thread.

Proof of correctness (Consistency
\Rightarrow
Satisfiability).

Given a completion
\sigma
,
we assign an arbitrary variable
x_{q}=T
iff in
\sigma
,

\mathtt{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.8}snd}(x_{q})
in
\tau_{\top}
is ordered before
\mathtt{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.8}snd}(\bar{x}_{q})
in
\tau_{\bot}
.
Now we prove this assignment makes
\psi
one-in-three satisfiable.

Firstly, we show some simple observations.
Because of the value
\alpha,\beta
,
one must execute
\mathtt{\color[rgb]{0,0.4,0}rcv}(C_{j})
in
\tau^{1}_{j}
before all events in
\tau_{j}^{2},\tau_{i,3},\tau_{i,4}
for all
i,j
.
Then we consider the value
\gamma
,
and notice that only
\tau_{i,1},\tau_{i,2}
send value
\gamma
once per thread.
This means,
in order to execute the events in
\tau_{i,3}
,
\tau_{i,4}
,
\tau_{j}^{2}
for all
i,j
,
we have to fully execute at least
n
threads among
\tau_{i,1},\tau_{i,2}
for all
i
.
For each fixed
i
,
we must execute exactly one of
\tau_{i,1}
,
\tau_{i,2}
,
because there is only one
\mathtt{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.8}snd}(x_{i}),\mathtt{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.8}snd}(\bar{x}_{i})
in
\tau_{\top},\tau_{\bot}
and the other two are in thread
\tau_{i,3},\tau_{i,4}
.

Secondly, we show
\psi
must be satisfied.
Given the observations above,
one must execute the sent event at least once with value
C_{j}
for all
1\leq j\leq m
before
\tau_{j}^{2},\tau_{i,3},\tau_{i,4}
can be executed.
By our assignment, this means for each clause
C_{j}
,
there is at least one variable in
C_{j}
being assigned true,
so that
C_{j}
is satisfied.

Thirdly, we show each clause is satisfied by exactly one variable.
If more than one variable are assigned to be true in
C_{j}
,
then value
C_{j}
must be sent more than once before
\tau_{j}^{2},\tau_{i,3},\tau_{i,4}
can be executed.
However, because of value
\gamma,\alpha
, we cannot immediately receive the second (or third)
C_{j}
value,
which makes the
VCh
instance not consistent.
Therefore, exactly one literal per clause is assigned to be true.
The same reasoning works for synchronous channel.
∎

Appendix C
Lower bounds for
VCh-rf

C.1.
Hardness with Asynchronous Channels of Capacity 1

See
4.1

Proof.

We prove each direction separately.

Correctness (
\textsf{VSC{-}read}\Rightarrow\textsf{{VCh}{-}rf}
).

If
VSC-read
instance
\mathcal{X}=\langle\mathsf{S},{\textsf{po}},{\textsf{rf}}\rangle
is consistent, then
VCh-rf
instance
\langle\mathcal{X}^{\prime},\mathsf{cap}^{\prime},{\textsf{rf}}^{\prime}\rangle
is consistent.
For a sequence of events
\pi=e_{1}\cdots e_{n}
in
\mathsf{S}
,
we define the mapping of
\pi
using
M
as
M(\pi)=M(e_{1})\cdots M(e_{n})
.
Let
\rho
be a linear sequence concretizing
\mathcal{X}
,
and we show
\sigma=M(\rho)
is the concretization of
\langle\mathcal{X}^{\prime},\mathsf{cap}^{\prime},{\textsf{rf}}^{\prime}\rangle
.
For convenience, we define the reverse map of
M
as
M^{-1}
,
where
M^{-1}(e)=f
iff
e
is in
M(f)
.
That is,
M^{-1}
maps a event
e
in
\mathsf{S}^{\prime}
back to the event
f\in\mathsf{S}
,
such that
e\in M(f)
.

Firstly, we argue that
\sigma
respects
{\textsf{po}}^{\prime}
.
Assuming
(e_{1},e_{2})\in{\textsf{po}}^{\prime}
and
e_{1}
is ordered after
e_{2}
in
\sigma
,
then there are two possible situations.
(1)
M^{-1}(e_{1})=M^{-1}(e_{2})=f
.
This is impossible,
because
\sigma
doesn’t reorder events in
M(f)
.
(2)
M^{-1}(e_{1})\neq M^{-1}(e_{2})
,
then by definition of
{\textsf{po}}^{\prime}
, we have
(M^{-1}(e_{1}),M^{-1}(e_{2}))\in{\textsf{po}}
.
In this case,
\sigma
should order
e_{1}
before
e_{2}
,
so that it’s also impossible.
Therefore,
\sigma
must respect
{\textsf{po}}^{\prime}
.

Secondly, we argue the
{\textsf{rf}}^{\prime}
is also satisfied.
Assuming there is a receive event
\mathtt{\color[rgb]{0,0.4,0}rcv}(\mathtt{ch})\in\mathsf{S}^{\prime}
,
it should observe
\mathtt{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.8}snd}(\mathtt{ch})
,
but turns out to observe the wrong send event
\mathtt{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.8}snd}^{\prime}(\mathtt{ch})
in
\sigma
.
First, we argue
\mathtt{ch}
cannot be
\ell
,
because for each event
e\in\mathsf{S}
,

M(e)
contains exactly one send and its receiver to
\ell
,
and
M(e)
doesn’t interleave with
M(e^{\prime})
in
\sigma
for all
e^{\prime}\neq e
.
Therefore,
\mathtt{ch}
can only be
\mathtt{ch}_{x}^{i}
for some register
x
and index
i
.
In this case,
since every
\mathtt{ch}_{x}^{i}
has capacity 1,
we claim there is no event
\mathsf{w}(x)
,
such that
M^{-1}(\mathtt{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.8}snd}^{\prime}(\mathtt{ch}))<^{\rho}_{\mathsf{\textsf{tr}}}\mathsf{w}(x)<^{\rho}_{\mathsf{\textsf{tr}}}M^{-1}(\mathtt{\color[rgb]{0,0.4,0}rcv}(\mathtt{ch}))
.
Otherwise, there will be two continuous send events to
\mathtt{ch}_{x}^{i}
.
Then
\rho
fails to meet the
rf
relation,
because
(M^{-1}(\mathtt{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.8}snd}(\mathtt{ch})),M^{-1}(\mathtt{\color[rgb]{0,0.4,0}rcv}(\mathtt{ch})))\in{\textsf{rf}}
,
but
M^{-1}(\mathtt{\color[rgb]{0,0.4,0}rcv}(\mathtt{ch}))
observes
M^{-1}(\mathtt{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.8}snd}^{\prime}(\mathtt{ch}))
in
\rho
,
which is impossible.

Lastly,
\sigma
is well-formed.
Indeed, by our construction,
for each write event
\mathsf{w}(x)\in\mathsf{S}
together with all read events observing
\mathsf{w}(x)
,
there will be exactly
m_{x}
send and receive events.
That is, we construct one send and receive event to each
\mathtt{ch}_{x}^{i}
.
After executing all of them,
\mathtt{ch}_{x}^{i}
will be empty again.
Since the reads-from relation is satisfied,
then
\sigma
should be well-formed.

Correctness (
\textsf{{VCh}{-}rf}\Rightarrow\textsf{VSC{-}read}
).

Secondly, if
\langle\mathcal{X}^{\prime},\mathsf{cap}^{\prime},{\textsf{rf}}^{\prime}\rangle
is consistent,
then
\mathcal{X}
is consistent.
Let
\sigma
be a concretization of
\langle\mathcal{X}^{\prime},\mathsf{cap}^{\prime},{\textsf{rf}}^{\prime}\rangle
,
we construct
\rho
as a concretization of
\mathcal{X}
as following.
We note that because of the channel
\ell
,
every event sequence
M(e)
should not interleave with each other for all
e\in\mathsf{S}
(see Figure
6(a)
for explanation).
Otherwise, there will be at least two continuous send events to channel
\ell
,
which only has capacity 1.
This implies we can map
\sigma
back into a serialized sequence
\rho
of
\mathsf{S}
,
s.t.
M(\rho)=\sigma
.
We argue
\rho
is a valid concretization of
{\mathcal{X}}
.

Firstly, we argue that
po
is satisfied.
Assuming
(e_{1},e_{2})\in{\textsf{po}}
and
e_{1}
is ordered after
e_{2}
in
\rho
,
then it implies
M(e_{1})
should be ordered after
M(e_{2})
in
\sigma
,
which violates
{\textsf{rf}}^{\prime}
.

Secondly, we argue
rf
is also satisfied.
Assuming
\mathsf{r}(x)
should observe
\mathsf{w}(x)
,
but it observes
\mathsf{w}^{\prime}(x)
in
\rho
,
we now consider the mapped event sequences in
\sigma
.
This implies one of the following two situations should happen.
(1)
M(\mathsf{w}(x))<^{\sigma}_{\mathsf{\textsf{tr}}}M(\mathsf{r}(x))
,
which violates
{\textsf{rf}}^{\prime}
.
(2)
M(\mathsf{w}(x))<^{\sigma}_{\mathsf{\textsf{tr}}}M(\mathsf{w}^{\prime}(x))<^{\sigma}_{\mathsf{\textsf{tr}}}M(\mathsf{r}(x))
.
In this case, there will be two continuous send events to some channel
\mathtt{ch}_{x}^{i}
,
which is impossible as well.
Therefore
rf
must be satisfied and thus
{\mathcal{X}}
is indeed consistent.
∎

C.2.
Hardness with
3
Threads and Small Channel Capacity

Here we show that
VCh-rf
is
NP
-hard already with
3
threads and maximum channel capacity
k\leq 2
.

Overview.

Our reduction is from the 3SAT problem, and constructs
a
VCh-rf
instance

\langle\mathcal{X},\mathsf{cap},{\textsf{rf}}\rangle
,
where
\mathcal{X}=\langle\mathsf{S},{\textsf{po}}\rangle

starting from a given 3CNF formula
\psi
,
such that
\langle\mathcal{X},\mathsf{cap},{\textsf{rf}}\rangle
is consistent iff
\psi
is satisfiable.
Let
\psi=C_{1}\land C_{2}\cdots C_{{n_{c}}}
be a conjunction of
{n_{c}}

clauses over
{n_{v}}
propositional variables
x_{1},\ldots,x_{n_{v}}
.
At a high level,
{\mathcal{X}}
is structured in
2

phases.
The first phase, divided into
{n_{v}}
sub-phases arranged sequentially,
picks an assignment for each variable
x_{i}
.
The second phase, divided into
{n_{c}}
sub-phases arranged sequentially,
encode the constraint that for clause
C_{j}
, the assignment to
at least one of three literals in
C_{j}
was picked to be true in the first phase.
Figure
17
shows the schema of our hardness construction.

x_{1}
\dots
x_{{n_{v}}}
C_{1}
\dots
C_{{n_{c}}}

\tau_{1}

\tau_{2}

\tau_{3}

(a)

Overall Scheme.

\mathtt{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.8}snd}(\alpha)
\mathtt{\color[rgb]{0,0.4,0}rcv}(\alpha)
\mathtt{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.8}snd}(\ell)
\mathtt{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.8}snd}_{\top}(\mathtt{ch}_{i}^{1})
\dots
\mathtt{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.8}snd}_{\top}(\mathtt{ch}_{i}^{f_{i}})
\mathtt{\color[rgb]{0,0.4,0}rcv}(\ell)
\mathtt{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.8}snd}(\alpha)
\mathtt{\color[rgb]{0,0.4,0}rcv}(\alpha)
\mathtt{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.8}snd}(\ell)
\mathtt{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.8}snd}_{\bot}(\mathtt{ch}_{i}^{1})
\dots
\mathtt{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.8}snd}_{\bot}(\mathtt{ch}_{i}^{f_{i}})
\mathtt{\color[rgb]{0,0.4,0}rcv}(\ell)
A^{r}_{i}

\tau_{1}

\tau_{2}

\tau_{3}

(b)

Sub-phase for variable
x_{i}
.

\mathtt{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.8}snd}(\beta_{1})
\mathtt{\color[rgb]{0,0.4,0}rcv}(\beta_{4})
\mathtt{\color[rgb]{0,0.4,0}rcv}_{\top}(\mathtt{ch}_{j_{1}}^{m_{1}})
\mathtt{\color[rgb]{0,0.4,0}rcv}_{\bot}(\mathtt{ch}_{j_{2}}^{m_{2}})
\mathtt{\color[rgb]{0,0.4,0}rcv}(\beta_{1})
\mathtt{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.8}snd}(\beta_{2})
\mathtt{\color[rgb]{0,0.4,0}rcv}(\beta_{3})
\mathtt{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.8}snd}(\beta_{4})
\mathtt{\color[rgb]{0,0.4,0}rcv}_{\top}(\mathtt{ch}_{j_{2}}^{m_{2}})
\mathtt{\color[rgb]{0,0.4,0}rcv}_{\bot}(\mathtt{ch}_{j_{3}}^{m_{3}})
\mathtt{\color[rgb]{0,0.4,0}rcv}(\beta_{2})
\mathtt{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.8}snd}(\beta_{3})
\mathtt{\color[rgb]{0,0.4,0}rcv}_{\top}(\mathtt{ch}_{j_{3}}^{m_{3}})
\mathtt{\color[rgb]{0,0.4,0}rcv}_{\bot}(\mathtt{ch}_{j_{1}}^{m_{1}})
B^{r}_{i}

\tau_{1}

\tau_{2}

\tau_{3}

(c)

Sub-phase for clause
C_{j}
.

Figure 17
.

Reduction from 3SAT to
VCh-rf
.
Here
\mathsf{cap}\mathsf{(\ell)}=\mathsf{cap}\mathsf{(\beta_{i})}=1
,
and
\mathsf{cap}\mathsf{(\alpha)}=\mathsf{cap}\mathsf{(\mathtt{ch}_{i}^{s})}=2
.
Reads-from relations are either depicted using red arrows or are described in texts.

Reduction.

The
VCh-rf
instance
\langle\mathcal{X},\mathsf{cap},{\textsf{rf}}\rangle
we construct
has
3
threads
\tau_{1},\tau_{2},\tau_{3}
.
It uses the following sets of distinct channels

\mathcal{C}_{1}\uplus\mathcal{C}_{2}
,
where
\mathcal{C}_{1}=\{\ell,\beta_{1},\beta_{2},\beta_{3},\beta_{4}\}

is the set of asynchronous channels with capacity
1
,
while
\mathcal{C}_{2}=\{\alpha\}\uplus\{\mathtt{ch}_{i}^{s}\,|\,1\leq s\leq f_{i}\}

is the set of asynchronous channels with capacity
2
,
where
f_{i}
denotes the number of occurrences of variable
x_{i}
in formula
\psi
,
and the channel
\mathtt{ch}_{i}^{s}
will represent the
s^{\text{th}}
occurrence of
x_{i}
.
For each thread
\tau_{r}
(
r\in\{1,2,3\}
),
the sequence
\rho_{r}
of events in
\tau_{r}
is of the form
\rho_{r}=A^{r}\cdot B^{r}
,
where
A^{r}
and
B^{r}
are sequences of events corresponding
to the first and second phases respectively and have the form

\displaystyle\begin{array}[]{rcl}A^{r}=A^{r}_{1}\cdot A^{r}_{2}\cdots A^{r}_{n_{v}}&&B^{r}=B^{r}_{1}\cdot B^{r}_{2}\cdots B^{r}_{n_{c}}\end{array}

The sequence
A^{r}_{i}
encodes some choice of assignments
to variable
x_{i}
.
Each
A^{r}_{i}
contains an atomic event sequence for
r=1,2

and the atomicity is guaranteed by channel
\ell
with capacity 1 (see Figure
6(a)
).
In particular,
A^{3}_{i}=\epsilon
is the empty sequence, while

A^{1}_{i}
and
A^{2}_{i}
are described next:

\displaystyle\begin{array}[]{rcl}A^{1}_{i}&=&\mathtt{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.8}snd}(\alpha)\cdot\mathtt{\color[rgb]{0,0.4,0}rcv}(\alpha)\cdot\mathtt{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.8}snd}(\ell)\cdot\mathtt{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.8}snd}_{\top}(\mathtt{ch}_{i}^{1})\cdots\mathtt{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.8}snd}_{\top}(\mathtt{ch}_{i}^{f_{i}})\cdot\mathtt{\color[rgb]{0,0.4,0}rcv}(\ell)\\
A^{2}_{i}&=&\mathtt{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.8}snd}(\alpha)\cdot\mathtt{\color[rgb]{0,0.4,0}rcv}(\alpha)\cdot\mathtt{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.8}snd}(\ell)\cdot\mathtt{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.8}snd}_{\bot}(\mathtt{ch}_{i}^{1})\cdots\mathtt{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.8}snd}_{\bot}(\mathtt{ch}_{i}^{f_{i}})\cdot\mathtt{\color[rgb]{0,0.4,0}rcv}(\ell)\end{array}

Consider the clause
C_{j}=\gamma_{1}\lor\gamma_{2}\lor\gamma_{3}
,
where
\gamma_{s}
is a literal over variable
x_{j_{s}}

(we assume
j_{1}<j_{2}<j_{3}
),
and let
C_{j}
be respectively the
m_{1}^{\text{th}}
,
m_{2}^{\text{th}}
and
m_{3}^{\text{th}}

occurrence of
x_{j_{1}},x_{j_{2}},x_{j_{3}}
in
\psi
.
Then,
B^{1}_{j},B^{2}_{j},B^{3}_{j}
are the following sequences corresponding to
C_{j}
in threads
\tau_{1},\tau_{2},\tau_{3}
respectively:

\displaystyle\begin{array}[]{rcl}B^{1}_{j}&=&\mathtt{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.8}snd}(\beta_{1})\cdot\mathtt{\color[rgb]{0,0.4,0}rcv}(\beta_{4})\cdot\mathtt{\color[rgb]{0,0.4,0}rcv}_{\top}(\mathtt{ch}^{m_{1}}_{j_{1}})\cdot\mathtt{\color[rgb]{0,0.4,0}rcv}_{\bot}(\mathtt{ch}^{m_{2}}_{j_{2}})\\
B^{2}_{j}&=&\mathtt{\color[rgb]{0,0.4,0}rcv}(\beta_{1})\cdot\mathtt{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.8}snd}(\beta_{2})\cdot\mathtt{\color[rgb]{0,0.4,0}rcv}(\beta_{3})\cdot\mathtt{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.8}snd}(\beta_{4})\cdot\mathtt{\color[rgb]{0,0.4,0}rcv}_{\top}(\mathtt{ch}^{m_{2}}_{j_{2}})\cdot\mathtt{\color[rgb]{0,0.4,0}rcv}_{\bot}(\mathtt{ch}^{m_{3}}_{j_{3}})\\
B^{3}_{j}&=&\mathtt{\color[rgb]{0,0.4,0}rcv}(\beta_{2})\cdot\mathtt{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.8}snd}(\beta_{3})\cdot\mathtt{\color[rgb]{0,0.4,0}rcv}_{\top}(\mathtt{ch}^{m_{3}}_{j_{3}})\cdot\mathtt{\color[rgb]{0,0.4,0}rcv}_{\bot}(\mathtt{ch}^{m_{1}}_{j_{1}})\end{array}

We now specify the reads-from relation:

•

\mathtt{\color[rgb]{0,0.4,0}rcv}(\ell)
in
A^{r}_{i}
observes
\mathtt{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.8}snd}(\ell)
in
A^{r}_{i}
(
r\in\{1,2\},i\in\{1,\ldots,{n_{v}}\}
).

•

\mathtt{\color[rgb]{0,0.4,0}rcv}(\alpha)
in
A^{r}_{i}
observes
\mathtt{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.8}snd}(\alpha)
in
A^{\bar{r}}_{i}
(
\{r,\bar{r}\}=\{1,2\},i\in\{1,\ldots,{n_{v}}\}
).
The events on channel
\alpha
thus ensure that all events (belonging to the first phase) of
x_{i}
will appear before those of
x_{i+1}
in any concretization.

•

\mathtt{\color[rgb]{0,0.4,0}rcv}(\beta_{s})
in
B^{r}_{j}
observes the event
\mathtt{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.8}snd}(\beta_{s})
in
B^{s}_{j}
(
s\in\{1,2,3,4\},r\in\{1,2\},j\in\{1,2,\ldots,{n_{c}}\}
).

•

Recall that in clause
C_{j}=\gamma_{1}\lor\gamma_{2}\lor\gamma_{3}

are such that the literal
\gamma_{p}
is either
x_{j_{p}}
or
\neg x_{j_{p}}
, and
C_{j}
is the
m_{p}^{\text{th}}

occurrence of
x_{j_{p}}
in
\psi
(
p\in\{1,2,3\}
).
In the former case (i.e.,
\gamma_{p}=x_{j_{p}}
),
we pair the receive events
\mathtt{\color[rgb]{0,0.4,0}rcv}_{\top}(\mathtt{ch}^{m_{p}}_{j_{p}})
and
\mathtt{\color[rgb]{0,0.4,0}rcv}_{\bot}(\mathtt{ch}^{m_{p}}_{j_{p}})

to the send events
\mathtt{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.8}snd}_{\top}(\mathtt{ch}^{m_{p}}_{j_{p}})
and
\mathtt{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.8}snd}_{\bot}(\mathtt{ch}^{m_{p}}_{j_{p}})
in
A^{1}_{j_{p}}
and
A^{2}_{j_{p}}
, respectively.
Otherwise (i.e.,
\gamma_{p}=\neg x_{j_{p}}
),
we pair the receive events
\mathtt{\color[rgb]{0,0.4,0}rcv}_{\top}(\mathtt{ch}^{m_{p}}_{j_{p}})
and
\mathtt{\color[rgb]{0,0.4,0}rcv}_{\bot}(\mathtt{ch}^{m_{p}}_{j_{p}})

to the send events
\mathtt{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.8}snd}_{\bot}(\mathtt{ch}^{m_{p}}_{j_{p}})
and
\mathtt{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.8}snd}_{\top}(\mathtt{ch}^{m_{p}}_{j_{p}})
in
A^{2}_{j_{p}}
and
A^{1}_{j_{p}}
, respectively.

The following lemma states the correctness of the above construction.

Lemma C.1.

\psi
is satisfiable iff
\langle\mathcal{X},\mathsf{cap},{\textsf{rf}}\rangle
is consistent.

Finally, the number of events in
\langle\mathcal{X},\mathsf{cap},{\textsf{rf}}\rangle
is
O({n_{v}}+{n_{c}})
,
which concludes case (ii) of Theorem
1.6
.

Proof.

We prove each direction separately.

Correctness (Satisfiability
\Rightarrow
Consistency).

If
\psi
is satisfiable, then there is a concretization
\sigma
,
and we sketch it as following.
In general,
\sigma=\sigma_{1}\circ\sigma_{2}
.
We first describe a linear sequence
\sigma_{1}
of first phase (
A^{r}_{i}
).
Then we describe the linear sequence
\sigma_{2}
of the second phase (
B^{r}_{j}
).
In general,
\sigma_{1}
is of the following form.

\sigma_{1}=A_{1}\circ A_{2}\circ\dots\circ A_{n_{v}}

where
A_{i}
is a linear sequence of
A^{1}_{i},A^{2}_{i}
in
\tau_{1},\tau_{2}
.
Here we use superscript to denote the thread each event belongs to.
If
x_{i}
is assigned to be true, then

\displaystyle A_{i}

\displaystyle=\mathtt{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.8}snd}^{\tau_{2}}(\alpha)\cdot\mathtt{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.8}snd}^{\tau_{1}}(\alpha)\cdot\mathtt{\color[rgb]{0,0.4,0}rcv}^{\tau_{1}}(\alpha)\cdot\mathtt{\color[rgb]{0,0.4,0}rcv}^{\tau_{2}}(\alpha)

\displaystyle\cdot\mathtt{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.8}snd}^{\tau_{1}}(\ell)\cdot\mathtt{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.8}snd}_{\top}^{\tau_{1}}(\mathtt{ch}_{i}^{1})\cdots\mathtt{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.8}snd}_{\top}^{\tau_{1}}(\mathtt{ch}_{i}^{f_{i}})\cdot\mathtt{\color[rgb]{0,0.4,0}rcv}^{\tau_{1}}(\ell)

\displaystyle\cdot\mathtt{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.8}snd}^{\tau_{2}}(\ell)\cdot\mathtt{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.8}snd}_{\bot}^{\tau_{2}}(\mathtt{ch}_{i}^{1})\cdots\mathtt{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.8}snd}_{\bot}^{\tau_{2}}(\mathtt{ch}_{i}^{f_{i}})\cdot\mathtt{\color[rgb]{0,0.4,0}rcv}^{\tau_{2}}(\ell)

If
x_{i}
is assigned to be false, then

\displaystyle A_{i}

\displaystyle=\mathtt{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.8}snd}^{\tau_{2}}(\alpha)\cdot\mathtt{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.8}snd}^{\tau_{1}}(\alpha)\cdot\mathtt{\color[rgb]{0,0.4,0}rcv}^{\tau_{1}}(\alpha)\cdot\mathtt{\color[rgb]{0,0.4,0}rcv}^{\tau_{2}}(\alpha)

\displaystyle\cdot\mathtt{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.8}snd}^{\tau_{2}}(\ell)\cdot\mathtt{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.8}snd}_{\bot}^{\tau_{2}}(\mathtt{ch}_{i}^{1})\cdots\mathtt{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.8}snd}_{\bot}^{\tau_{2}}(\mathtt{ch}_{i}^{f_{i}})\cdot\mathtt{\color[rgb]{0,0.4,0}rcv}^{\tau_{2}}(\ell)

\displaystyle\cdot\mathtt{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.8}snd}^{\tau_{1}}(\ell)\cdot\mathtt{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.8}snd}_{\top}^{\tau_{1}}(\mathtt{ch}_{i}^{1})\cdots\mathtt{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.8}snd}_{\top}^{\tau_{1}}(\mathtt{ch}_{i}^{f_{i}})\cdot\mathtt{\color[rgb]{0,0.4,0}rcv}^{\tau_{1}}(\ell)

One can easily verify these two concretizations satisfy the reads-from relation within each
A^{r}_{i}
.
Now we turn to
\sigma_{2}
and

\sigma_{2}
is of the following pattern.

\sigma_{2}=B_{1}\circ\dots\circ B_{n_{c}}

where
B_{j}
is a linear sequence of all events in
B^{r}_{j}
for all
1\leq r\leq 3
.

B_{j}
depends on the value of each literal in
C_{j}=\gamma_{1}\lor\gamma_{2}\lor\gamma_{3}
.
Since
C_{j}
is satisfied, there exists one literal to be true.
Without loss of generality, we assume
\gamma_{1}
is true (other cases can be solved similarly).
Then we have four possibilities, as the value of
\gamma_{2},\gamma_{3}
can be either true of false.

•

If
\gamma_{2}=
true and
\gamma_{3}
= true, then

B_{j}=\mathtt{\color[rgb]{0,0.4,0}rcv}_{\top}(\mathtt{ch}^{m_{1}}_{j_{1}})\cdot\mathtt{\color[rgb]{0,0.4,0}rcv}_{\top}(\mathtt{ch}^{m_{2}}_{j_{2}})\cdot\mathtt{\color[rgb]{0,0.4,0}rcv}_{\top}(\mathtt{ch}^{m_{3}}_{j_{3}})\cdot\mathtt{\color[rgb]{0,0.4,0}rcv}_{\bot}(\mathtt{ch}^{m_{1}}_{j_{1}})\cdot\mathtt{\color[rgb]{0,0.4,0}rcv}_{\bot}(\mathtt{ch}^{m_{2}}_{j_{2}})\cdot\mathtt{\color[rgb]{0,0.4,0}rcv}_{\bot}(\mathtt{ch}^{m_{3}}_{j_{3}})

•

If
\gamma_{2}=
true and
\gamma_{3}
= false, then

B_{j}=\mathtt{\color[rgb]{0,0.4,0}rcv}_{\top}(\mathtt{ch}^{m_{1}}_{j_{1}})\cdot\mathtt{\color[rgb]{0,0.4,0}rcv}_{\top}(\mathtt{ch}^{m_{2}}_{j_{2}})\cdot\mathtt{\color[rgb]{0,0.4,0}rcv}_{\bot}(\mathtt{ch}^{m_{3}}_{j_{3}})\cdot\mathtt{\color[rgb]{0,0.4,0}rcv}_{\top}(\mathtt{ch}^{m_{3}}_{j_{3}})\cdot\mathtt{\color[rgb]{0,0.4,0}rcv}_{\bot}(\mathtt{ch}^{m_{1}}_{j_{1}})\cdot\mathtt{\color[rgb]{0,0.4,0}rcv}_{\bot}(\mathtt{ch}^{m_{2}}_{j_{2}})

•

If
\gamma_{2}=
false and
\gamma_{3}
= true, then

B_{j}=\mathtt{\color[rgb]{0,0.4,0}rcv}_{\top}(\mathtt{ch}^{m_{1}}_{j_{1}})\cdot\mathtt{\color[rgb]{0,0.4,0}rcv}_{\bot}(\mathtt{ch}^{m_{2}}_{j_{2}})\cdot\mathtt{\color[rgb]{0,0.4,0}rcv}_{\top}(\mathtt{ch}^{m_{2}}_{j_{2}})\cdot\mathtt{\color[rgb]{0,0.4,0}rcv}_{\top}(\mathtt{ch}^{m_{3}}_{j_{3}})\cdot\mathtt{\color[rgb]{0,0.4,0}rcv}_{\bot}(\mathtt{ch}^{m_{3}}_{j_{3}})\cdot\mathtt{\color[rgb]{0,0.4,0}rcv}_{\bot}(\mathtt{ch}^{m_{1}}_{j_{1}})

•

If
\gamma_{2}=
false and
\gamma_{3}
= false, then

B_{j}=\mathtt{\color[rgb]{0,0.4,0}rcv}_{\top}(\mathtt{ch}^{m_{1}}_{j_{1}})\cdot\mathtt{\color[rgb]{0,0.4,0}rcv}_{\bot}(\mathtt{ch}^{m_{2}}_{j_{2}})\cdot\mathtt{\color[rgb]{0,0.4,0}rcv}_{\top}(\mathtt{ch}^{m_{2}}_{j_{2}})\cdot\mathtt{\color[rgb]{0,0.4,0}rcv}_{\bot}(\mathtt{ch}^{m_{3}3}_{j_{3}})\cdot\mathtt{\color[rgb]{0,0.4,0}rcv}_{\top}(\mathtt{ch}^{m_{3}}_{j_{3}})\cdot\mathtt{\color[rgb]{0,0.4,0}rcv}_{\bot}(\mathtt{ch}^{m_{1}}_{j_{1}})

One can easily verify each
B_{j}
satisfies
po
and
we show they also satisfy the reads-from relation for channel
\mathtt{ch}_{j_{l}}^{m_{l}}
.

•

If
\gamma_{l}=x_{j_{l}}
and
x_{j_{l}}
is assigned to be true, then the send to
\mathtt{ch}_{j_{l}}^{m_{l}}
in
A^{1}_{{j_{l}}}
gets ordered before the send in
A^{2}_{{j_{l}}}
,
which is inline with
\mathtt{\color[rgb]{0,0.4,0}rcv}_{\top}(\mathtt{ch}_{j_{l}}^{m_{l}})
getting ordered before
\mathtt{\color[rgb]{0,0.4,0}rcv}_{\bot}(\mathtt{ch}_{j_{l}}^{m_{l}})
.

•

If
\gamma_{l}=x_{j_{l}}
and
x_{j_{l}}
is assigned to be false, then the send to
\mathtt{ch}_{j_{l}}^{m_{l}}
in
A^{1}_{{j_{l}}}
gets ordered after the send in
A^{2}_{{j_{l}}}
,
which is inline with
\mathtt{\color[rgb]{0,0.4,0}rcv}_{\top}(\mathtt{ch}_{j_{l}}^{m_{l}})
getting ordered after
\mathtt{\color[rgb]{0,0.4,0}rcv}_{\bot}(\mathtt{ch}_{j_{l}}^{m_{l}})
.

•

If
\gamma_{l}=\neg x_{j_{l}}
and
x_{j_{l}}
is assigned to be true, then the send to
\mathtt{ch}_{j_{l}}^{m_{l}}
in
A^{1}_{{j_{l}}}
gets ordered after the send in
A^{2}_{{j_{l}}}
,
which is inline with
\mathtt{\color[rgb]{0,0.4,0}rcv}_{\top}(\mathtt{ch}_{j_{l}}^{m_{l}})
getting ordered after
\mathtt{\color[rgb]{0,0.4,0}rcv}_{\bot}(\mathtt{ch}_{j_{l}}^{m_{l}})
.

•

If
\gamma_{l}=\neg x_{j_{l}}
and
x_{j_{l}}
is assigned to be false, then the send to
\mathtt{ch}_{j_{l}}^{m_{l}}
in
A^{1}_{{j_{l}}}
gets ordered before the send in
A^{2}_{{j_{l}}}
,
which is inline with
\mathtt{\color[rgb]{0,0.4,0}rcv}_{\top}(\mathtt{ch}_{j_{l}}^{m_{l}})
getting ordered before
\mathtt{\color[rgb]{0,0.4,0}rcv}_{\bot}(\mathtt{ch}_{j_{l}}^{m_{l}})
.

Therefore, the relative order of receive events to
\mathtt{ch}_{j_{l}}^{m_{l}}
in the second phase matches the send events to
\mathtt{ch}_{j_{l}}^{m_{l}}
in the first phase
and
\sigma
is a valid concretization.

Correctness (Consistency
\Rightarrow
Satisfiability).

Now we show the reverse direction,
i.e. if there is a concretization
\rho
,
then
\psi
can be satisfied.
First we construct the valuation function for each variable
x_{i}
and
then proceed to show this assignment makes
\psi
true.

We consider the events in the first phase,
and can notice that for an arbitrary fixed
1\leq j\leq n
,

\mathtt{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.8}snd}_{\top}(\mathtt{ch}_{j}^{1})
in
\tau_{1}
gets ordered before
\mathtt{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.8}snd}_{\bot}(\mathtt{ch}_{j}^{1})
in
\tau_{2}
,
iff
