\mathtt{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.8}snd}_{\top}(\mathtt{ch}_{j}^{k})
in
\tau_{1}
gets ordered before
\mathtt{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.8}snd}_{\bot}(\mathtt{ch}_{j}^{k})
in
\tau_{2}
for all
1\leq k\leq f_{j}
.
This is because the channel
\ell
behaves like a lock,
so that
\mathtt{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.8}snd}(\mathtt{ch}_{j}^{k})
in
\tau_{1},\tau_{2}
must be executed atomically (see Figure
6(a)
for explanation).
Our valuation function will assign
x_{i}=
true iff in
\rho
,
\mathtt{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.8}snd}_{\top}(\mathtt{ch}_{j}^{1})
in
\tau_{1}
gets ordered before
\mathtt{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.8}snd}_{\bot}(\mathtt{ch}_{j}^{1})
in
\tau_{2}
.

Now we proceed to show this assignment makes
\psi
true.
That is, we need to prove each clause
C_{j}
is satisfied.
By our encoding, the concretization of each clause is sequential,
i.e. for any possible concretization,
all events encoded for
C_{j}
must be executed before events encoded for
C_{j^{\prime}}
, s.t.
j^{\prime}>j
.
Events from different clauses cannot overlap,
because of channels
\beta_{1},\beta_{2},\beta_{3},\beta_{4}
.
Then we pick an arbitrary clause
C_{j}
and prove it is satisfied.

For the encoding of
C_{j}
,
the
rf
ensures that if
\mathtt{\color[rgb]{0,0.4,0}rcv}_{\top}(\mathtt{ch}^{m_{l}}_{j_{l}})
is ordered before
\mathtt{\color[rgb]{0,0.4,0}rcv}_{\bot}(\mathtt{ch}^{m_{l}}_{j_{l}})
in
\sigma
,
then the literal
\gamma_{l}
corresponding to
x_{j_{l}}
in clause
C_{j}
will be true and otherwise false.
We also guarantee that at least one of
\mathtt{\color[rgb]{0,0.4,0}rcv}_{\top}(\mathtt{ch}^{m_{l}}_{j_{l}})
will be before
\mathtt{\color[rgb]{0,0.4,0}rcv}_{\bot}(\mathtt{ch}^{m_{l}}_{j_{l}})
in
\sigma
,
otherwise,
\sigma
violates program order.
Therefore, there is at least one literal in
C_{j}
being assigned true and
C_{j}
is satisfied.
This completes the reduction.
∎

C.3.
Proof for Section
4.3

See
4.2

Proof.

We prove each direction separately.

Proof of correctness (Consistency
\Rightarrow
Satisfiability).

Assuming there is a valid concretization
\sigma
,
we construct a valuation function that satisfies
\psi
,
by checking the relative order of events in the first phase.
For variable
x_{i}
, we consider events in
I^{1}_{i},I^{2}_{i}
,
and assign
x_{i}
to be true iff
\mathtt{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.8}snd}_{\top}^{i}(\mathtt{ch}_{1})<^{\sigma}_{\mathsf{\textsf{tr}}}\mathtt{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.8}snd}_{\bot}^{i}(\mathtt{ch}_{1})
.
Now we prove this assignment makes an arbitrary clause true.
Without loss of generality, we show an arbitrary clause
C_{j}
can be satisfied.
We assume
C_{j}=\gamma_{1}\wedge\gamma_{2}\wedge\gamma_{3}
, and
the variable in
\gamma_{1},\gamma_{2},\gamma_{3}
are
x_{j_{1}},x_{j_{2}},x_{j_{3}}
.

The outline of the proof is the following.
We have an observation that in
\sigma
,
for an arbitrary
q\in\{1,2,3\}
,
\mathtt{\color[rgb]{0,0.4,0}rcv}_{\top}(c_{q})<^{\sigma}_{\mathsf{\textsf{tr}}}\mathtt{\color[rgb]{0,0.4,0}rcv}_{\bot}(c_{q})
iff
\gamma_{q}
is true.
Given the fact that this observation holds,
if all
\gamma_{q}
are false,
then
\mathtt{\color[rgb]{0,0.4,0}rcv}_{\bot}(c_{q})<^{\sigma}_{\mathsf{\textsf{tr}}}\mathtt{\color[rgb]{0,0.4,0}rcv}_{\top}(c_{q})
holds for all
q\in\{1,2,3\}
.
In this case,
\sigma
is not a valid concretization, as it violates
po
.
Therefore, we have at least one of
\gamma_{1},\gamma_{2},\gamma_{3}
is true, which satisfies
C_{j}
.
Now in the following content,
we prove
\mathtt{\color[rgb]{0,0.4,0}rcv}_{\top}(c_{q})<^{\sigma}_{\mathsf{\textsf{tr}}}\mathtt{\color[rgb]{0,0.4,0}rcv}_{\bot}(c_{q})
iff
\gamma_{q}
is true,
and we first prove the following two lemmas.

Firstly, we show that, for all
1\leq i\leq{n_{v}}
,
in
I^{1}_{i}
and
I^{2}_{i}
,

\mathtt{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.8}snd}_{\bot}^{i}(\mathtt{ch}_{1})<^{\sigma}_{\mathsf{\textsf{tr}}}\mathtt{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.8}snd}_{\top}^{i}(\mathtt{ch}_{1})
iff
\mathtt{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.8}snd}_{\bot}^{i}(\mathtt{ch}_{2})<^{\sigma}_{\mathsf{\textsf{tr}}}\mathtt{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.8}snd}_{\top}^{i}(\mathtt{ch}_{2})
.
If
\mathtt{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.8}snd}_{\bot}^{i}(\mathtt{ch}_{1})<^{\sigma}_{\mathsf{\textsf{tr}}}\mathtt{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.8}snd}_{\top}^{i}(\mathtt{ch}_{1})
in
I^{1}_{i},I^{2}_{i}
,
then we have
\mathtt{\color[rgb]{0,0.4,0}rcv}_{\bot}^{i}(\mathtt{ch}_{1})
in
A^{1}_{1,i}
is ordered before
\mathtt{\color[rgb]{0,0.4,0}rcv}_{\top}^{i}(\mathtt{ch}_{1})
in
A^{2}_{1,i}
.
As
\mathtt{\color[rgb]{0,0.4,0}rcv}_{\bot}^{i}(\mathtt{ch}_{2})\leq^{\sigma}_{\mathsf{\textsf{po}}}\mathtt{\color[rgb]{0,0.4,0}rcv}_{\bot}^{i}(\mathtt{ch}_{1})
in
A^{1}_{1,i}
,
and
\mathtt{\color[rgb]{0,0.4,0}rcv}_{\top}^{i}(\mathtt{ch}_{1})\leq^{\sigma}_{\mathsf{\textsf{po}}}\mathtt{\color[rgb]{0,0.4,0}rcv}_{\top}^{i}(\mathtt{ch}_{2})
in
A^{2}_{1,i}
,
by transitivity, we have
\mathtt{\color[rgb]{0,0.4,0}rcv}_{\bot}^{i}(\mathtt{ch}_{2})<^{\sigma}_{\mathsf{\textsf{tr}}}\mathtt{\color[rgb]{0,0.4,0}rcv}_{\top}^{i}(\mathtt{ch}_{2})
,
and thus
\mathtt{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.8}snd}_{\bot}^{i}(\mathtt{ch}_{2})<^{\sigma}_{\mathsf{\textsf{tr}}}\mathtt{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.8}snd}_{\top}^{i}(\mathtt{ch}_{2})
in
I^{1}_{i},I^{2}_{i}
.
In the other direction, if
\mathtt{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.8}snd}_{\top}^{i}(\mathtt{ch}_{1})<^{\sigma}_{\mathsf{\textsf{tr}}}\mathtt{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.8}snd}_{\bot}^{i}(\mathtt{ch}_{1})
in
I^{1}_{i},I^{2}_{i}
,
then since
\mathtt{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.8}snd}_{\bot}^{i}(\mathtt{ch}_{1})\leq^{\sigma}_{\mathsf{\textsf{po}}}\mathtt{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.8}snd}_{\bot}^{i}(\mathtt{ch}_{2})
in
I^{1}_{i}
,
and
\mathtt{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.8}snd}_{\top}^{i}(\mathtt{ch}_{2})
is program ordered before
\mathtt{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.8}snd}_{\top}^{i}(\mathtt{ch}_{1})
in
I^{2}_{i}
,
we have
\mathtt{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.8}snd}_{\top}^{i}(\mathtt{ch}_{2})<^{\sigma}_{\mathsf{\textsf{tr}}}\mathtt{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.8}snd}_{\bot}^{i}(\mathtt{ch}_{2})
by transitivity.

The same reasoning above can be used to show in
A^{1}_{j,l}
and
A^{2}_{j,l}
,

\mathtt{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.8}snd}_{\bot}^{l}(\mathtt{ch}_{1})<^{\sigma}_{\mathsf{\textsf{tr}}}\mathtt{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.8}snd}_{\top}^{l}(\mathtt{ch}_{1})
iff
\mathtt{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.8}snd}_{\bot}^{l}(\mathtt{ch}_{2})<^{\sigma}_{\mathsf{\textsf{tr}}}\mathtt{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.8}snd}_{\top}^{l}(\mathtt{ch}_{2})

for all
j
, where
1\leq j\leq{n_{c}}
.

Secondly, we show that for all
j
, s.t.
1\leq j\leq{n_{c}}
,
in
A^{1}_{j,l}
and
A^{2}_{j,l}
,

\mathtt{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.8}snd}^{l}_{\top}(\mathtt{ch}_{1})<^{\sigma}_{\mathsf{\textsf{tr}}}\mathtt{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.8}snd}^{l}_{\bot}(\mathtt{ch}_{1})
iff in
A^{1}_{j-1,l}
and
A^{2}_{j-1,l}
,

\mathtt{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.8}snd}^{l}_{\top}(\mathtt{ch}_{1})<^{\sigma}_{\mathsf{\textsf{tr}}}\mathtt{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.8}snd}^{l}_{\bot}(\mathtt{ch}_{1})
.
That is, the assignments for values are consistent across phases.
The reasoning is similar to previous one.
If
\mathtt{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.8}snd}^{l}_{\top}(\mathtt{ch}_{1})<^{\sigma}_{\mathsf{\textsf{tr}}}\mathtt{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.8}snd}^{l}_{\bot}(\mathtt{ch}_{1})
in
A^{1}_{j-1,l}
and
A^{2}_{j-1,l}
,
then we consider the events in
A^{1}_{j,l}
and
A^{2}_{j,l}
.
If
\mathtt{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.8}snd}^{l}_{\top}(\mathtt{ch}_{1})<^{\sigma}_{\mathsf{\textsf{tr}}}\mathtt{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.8}snd}^{l}_{\bot}(\mathtt{ch}_{1})
,
since
\mathtt{\color[rgb]{0,0.4,0}rcv}^{l}_{\top}(\mathtt{ch}_{1})\leq^{\sigma}_{\mathsf{\textsf{po}}}\mathtt{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.8}snd}^{l}_{\top}(\mathtt{ch}_{1})

and
\mathtt{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.8}snd}^{l}_{\bot}(\mathtt{ch}_{1})\leq^{\sigma}_{\mathsf{\textsf{po}}}\mathtt{\color[rgb]{0,0.4,0}rcv}^{l}_{\bot}(\mathtt{ch}_{1})
,
by transitivity,
we have
\mathtt{\color[rgb]{0,0.4,0}rcv}^{l}_{\top}(\mathtt{ch}_{1})<^{\sigma}_{\mathsf{\textsf{tr}}}\mathtt{\color[rgb]{0,0.4,0}rcv}^{l}_{\bot}(\mathtt{ch}_{1})
.
Therefore, we have in
A^{1}_{j-1,l}
and
A^{2}_{j-1,l}
,

\mathtt{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.8}snd}^{l}_{\top}(\mathtt{ch}_{1})<^{\sigma}_{\mathsf{\textsf{tr}}}\mathtt{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.8}snd}^{l}_{\bot}(\mathtt{ch}_{1})
.
In the other direction, if in
A^{1}_{j-1,l}
and
A^{2}_{j-1,l}
,

\mathtt{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.8}snd}^{l}_{\top}(\mathtt{ch}_{1})<^{\sigma}_{\mathsf{\textsf{tr}}}\mathtt{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.8}snd}^{l}_{\bot}(\mathtt{ch}_{1})
,
then we have
\mathtt{\color[rgb]{0,0.4,0}rcv}^{l}_{\top}(\mathtt{ch}_{1})<^{\sigma}_{\mathsf{\textsf{tr}}}\mathtt{\color[rgb]{0,0.4,0}rcv}^{l}_{\bot}(\mathtt{ch}_{1})
in
A^{1}_{j,l},A^{2}_{j,l}
.
As
\mathtt{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.8}snd}^{i}_{\top}(\mathtt{ch}_{2})\leq^{\sigma}_{\mathsf{\textsf{po}}}\mathtt{\color[rgb]{0,0.4,0}rcv}^{l}_{\top}(\mathtt{ch}_{1})
,
and
\mathtt{\color[rgb]{0,0.4,0}rcv}^{l}_{\top}(\mathtt{ch}_{1})\leq^{\sigma}_{\mathsf{\textsf{po}}}\mathtt{\color[rgb]{0,0.4,0}rcv}^{l}_{\bot}(\mathtt{ch}_{1})
,
by transitivity, we have
\mathtt{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.8}snd}^{l}_{\top}(\mathtt{ch}_{2})<^{\sigma}_{\mathsf{\textsf{tr}}}\mathtt{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.8}snd}^{l}_{\bot}(\mathtt{ch}_{2})
in
A^{1}_{j,l},A^{2}_{j,l}
.
Then following the lemma we proved previously,
we have in
A^{1}_{j,l}
and
A^{2}_{j,l}
,

\mathtt{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.8}snd}^{l}_{\top}(\mathtt{ch}_{1})<^{\sigma}_{\mathsf{\textsf{tr}}}\mathtt{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.8}snd}^{l}_{\bot}(\mathtt{ch}_{1})
.
Intuitively, this observation captures the fact that
our valuation for every variable is properly maintained across phases.
That is, in each phase,
we will copy the valuation once and use copied valuation to satisfy the clause constraints.

Combining these two lemmas,
we prove that in each phase,

\mathtt{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.8}snd}_{\bot}(c_{q})<^{\sigma}_{\mathsf{\textsf{tr}}}\mathtt{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.8}snd}_{\top}(c_{q})
iff
x_{j_{q}}
= false.
If
\mathtt{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.8}snd}_{\bot}(c_{q})<^{\sigma}_{\mathsf{\textsf{tr}}}\mathtt{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.8}snd}_{\top}(c_{q})
, then by transitivity,
we have
\mathtt{\color[rgb]{0,0.4,0}rcv}_{\bot}(\mathtt{ch}_{2})<^{\sigma}_{\mathsf{\textsf{tr}}}\mathtt{\color[rgb]{0,0.4,0}rcv}_{\top}(\mathtt{ch}_{2})
in
A^{1}_{j,j_{q}},A^{2}_{j,j_{q}}
,
which means
\mathtt{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.8}snd}_{\bot}^{i}(\mathtt{ch}_{1})<^{\sigma}_{\mathsf{\textsf{tr}}}\mathtt{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.8}snd}_{\top}^{i}(\mathtt{ch}_{1})
in
I^{1}_{i},I^{2}_{i}
,
so that
x_{j_{q}}
= false.
In the other direction,
if
x_{j_{q}}
= false, then
\mathtt{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.8}snd}_{\bot}^{i}(\mathtt{ch}_{1})<^{\sigma}_{\mathsf{\textsf{tr}}}\mathtt{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.8}snd}_{\top}^{i}(\mathtt{ch}_{1})
in
I^{1}_{i},I^{2}_{i}
.
By the previous lemmas, we have
\mathtt{\color[rgb]{0,0.4,0}rcv}_{\bot}^{j_{q}}(\mathtt{ch}_{1})<^{\sigma}_{\mathsf{\textsf{tr}}}\mathtt{\color[rgb]{0,0.4,0}rcv}_{\top}^{j_{q}}(\mathtt{ch}_{1})
in
A^{1}_{j,j_{q}},A^{2}_{j,j_{q}}

and thus
\mathtt{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.8}snd}_{\bot}(c_{q})<^{\sigma}_{\mathsf{\textsf{tr}}}\mathtt{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.8}snd}_{\top}(c_{q})
.

Then we are ready to show that
\mathtt{\color[rgb]{0,0.4,0}rcv}_{\top}(c_{q})<^{\sigma}_{\mathsf{\textsf{tr}}}\mathtt{\color[rgb]{0,0.4,0}rcv}_{\bot}(c_{q})
iff
\gamma_{q}
is true.
If
\gamma_{q}=x_{j_{q}}
, then
\mathtt{\color[rgb]{0,0.4,0}rcv}_{\top}(c_{q}),\mathtt{\color[rgb]{0,0.4,0}rcv}_{\bot}(c_{q})
observe
\mathtt{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.8}snd}_{\top}(c_{q}),\mathtt{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.8}snd}_{\bot}(c_{q})
, respectively.
Since
\mathtt{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.8}snd}_{\bot}(c_{q})<^{\sigma}_{\mathsf{\textsf{tr}}}\mathtt{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.8}snd}_{\top}(c_{q})
iff
x_{j_{q}}
= false and
\gamma_{q}
is true iff
x_{j_{q}}
is true,
we have
\mathtt{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.8}snd}_{\top}(c_{q})<^{\sigma}_{\mathsf{\textsf{tr}}}\mathtt{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.8}snd}_{\bot}(c_{q})
iff
\gamma_{q}
is true
and thus
\mathtt{\color[rgb]{0,0.4,0}rcv}_{\top}(c_{q})<^{\sigma}_{\mathsf{\textsf{tr}}}\mathtt{\color[rgb]{0,0.4,0}rcv}_{\bot}(c_{q})
iff
\gamma_{q}
is true.
Otherwise, if
\gamma_{q}=\neg x_{j_{q}}
,
then
\mathtt{\color[rgb]{0,0.4,0}rcv}_{\top}(c_{q}),\mathtt{\color[rgb]{0,0.4,0}rcv}_{\bot}(c_{q})
observe
\mathtt{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.8}snd}_{\bot}(c_{q}),\mathtt{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.8}snd}_{\top}(c_{q})
, respectively.
Since
\mathtt{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.8}snd}_{\bot}(c_{q})<^{\sigma}_{\mathsf{\textsf{tr}}}\mathtt{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.8}snd}_{\top}(c_{q})
iff
x_{j_{q}}
= false and
\gamma_{q}
is true iff
x_{j_{q}}
is false,
we have
\mathtt{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.8}snd}_{\bot}(c_{q})<^{\sigma}_{\mathsf{\textsf{tr}}}\mathtt{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.8}snd}_{\top}(c_{q})
iff
\gamma_{q}
is true
and thus
\mathtt{\color[rgb]{0,0.4,0}rcv}_{\top}(c_{q})<^{\sigma}_{\mathsf{\textsf{tr}}}\mathtt{\color[rgb]{0,0.4,0}rcv}_{\bot}(c_{q})
iff
\gamma_{q}
is true.

This completes one direction of the reduction.

Proof of correctness (Satisfiability
\Rightarrow
Consistency).

Now assuming there is a valuation function which satisfies
\psi
,
we construct a valid concretization
\sigma
.
In general,
\sigma
is of the following pattern.

\sigma=I\circ A_{1}\circ\dots\circ A_{{n_{c}}}

where
I
is a linear sequence of
I^{1},I^{2},I^{3}
and
A_{i}
is a linear sequence of
A^{1}_{i},A^{2}_{i},A^{3}_{i}
for all
1\leq i\leq{n_{c}}
.
We first discuss the details of
I
.

I=I_{1}\circ\dots I_{{n_{v}}}

where
I_{j}
is a linear sequence of
I^{1}_{j}
and
I^{2}_{j}
.
If
x_{j}
is assigned to be true, then

I_{j}=\mathtt{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.8}snd}_{\bot}^{j}(\mathtt{ch}_{1})\cdot\mathtt{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.8}snd}_{\bot}^{j}(\mathtt{ch}_{2})\cdot\mathtt{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.8}snd}_{\top}^{j}(\mathtt{ch}_{2})\cdot\mathtt{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.8}snd}_{\top}^{j}(\mathtt{ch}_{1})

If
x_{j}
is assigned to be false, then

I_{j}=\mathtt{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.8}snd}_{\top}^{j}(\mathtt{ch}_{2})\cdot\mathtt{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.8}snd}_{\top}^{j}(\mathtt{ch}_{1})\cdot\mathtt{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.8}snd}_{\bot}^{j}(\mathtt{ch}_{1})\cdot\mathtt{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.8}snd}_{\bot}^{j}(\mathtt{ch}_{2})

It is obvious that the program order is satisfied.
Next we discuss the details of
A_{j}
.
Generally, all
A_{j}
are of the following pattern.

A_{j}=A_{j,1}\circ\dots\circ A_{j,n}\circ B_{j}

where
A_{j,l}
is a linear sequence of
A^{1}_{j,l},A^{2}_{j,l}

and
B_{j}
is a linear sequence of
B^{1}_{j},B^{2}_{j},B^{3}_{j}
.
If
x_{l}
is assigned to be true, then
A_{j,l}
is of the following form.

\displaystyle A_{j,l}

\displaystyle=\mathtt{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.8}snd}_{\top}^{l}(\mathtt{ch}_{2})\cdot\mathtt{\color[rgb]{0,0.4,0}rcv}_{\top}^{l}(\mathtt{ch}_{1})\cdot{\mathtt{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.8}snd}_{\top}(c_{q})}\cdot\mathtt{\color[rgb]{0,0.4,0}rcv}_{\top}^{l}(\mathtt{ch}_{2})\cdot\mathtt{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.8}snd}_{\top}^{l}(\mathtt{ch}_{1})

\displaystyle\cdot\mathtt{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.8}snd}_{\bot}^{l}(\mathtt{ch}_{1})\cdot\mathtt{\color[rgb]{0,0.4,0}rcv}_{\bot}^{l}(\mathtt{ch}_{2})\cdot{\mathtt{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.8}snd}_{\bot}(c_{q})}\cdot\mathtt{\color[rgb]{0,0.4,0}rcv}_{\bot}^{l}(\mathtt{ch}_{1})\cdot\mathtt{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.8}snd}_{\bot}^{l}(\mathtt{ch}_{2})

where
\mathtt{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.8}snd}_{\top}(c_{q}),\mathtt{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.8}snd}_{\bot}(c_{q})
exist iff
x_{l}
appears as the
q
-th literal of clause
C_{j}
.
Otherwise, if
x_{l}
is assigned to be false, then

\displaystyle A_{j,l}

\displaystyle=\mathtt{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.8}snd}_{\bot}^{l}(\mathtt{ch}_{1})\cdot\mathtt{\color[rgb]{0,0.4,0}rcv}_{\bot}^{l}(\mathtt{ch}_{2})\cdot{\mathtt{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.8}snd}_{\bot}(c_{q})}\cdot\mathtt{\color[rgb]{0,0.4,0}rcv}_{\bot}^{l}(\mathtt{ch}_{1})\cdot\mathtt{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.8}snd}_{\bot}^{l}(\mathtt{ch}_{2})

\displaystyle\cdot\mathtt{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.8}snd}_{\top}^{l}(\mathtt{ch}_{2})\cdot\mathtt{\color[rgb]{0,0.4,0}rcv}_{\top}^{l}(\mathtt{ch}_{1})\cdot{\mathtt{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.8}snd}_{\top}(c_{q})}\cdot\mathtt{\color[rgb]{0,0.4,0}rcv}_{\top}^{l}(\mathtt{ch}_{2})\cdot\mathtt{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.8}snd}_{\top}^{l}(\mathtt{ch}_{1})

where
\mathtt{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.8}snd}_{\top}(c_{q}),\mathtt{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.8}snd}_{\bot}(c_{q})
exists if
x_{l}
appears as the
q
-th literal of
C_{j}
.
Finally, we discuss the linear sequence of
B^{1}_{j},B^{2}_{j},B^{3}_{j}
.
Since
C_{j}=\gamma_{1}\wedge\gamma_{2}\wedge\gamma_{3}
is satisfied, then at least one literal will be true.
Without loss of generality,
we assume
\gamma_{1}
= true.
Then we have four possible situations, depending on the value of
\gamma_{2}
and
\gamma_{3}
.

If
\gamma_{2}
= true and
\gamma_{3}
= true, then

C_{j}=\mathtt{\color[rgb]{0,0.4,0}rcv}_{\top}(c_{1})\cdot\mathtt{\color[rgb]{0,0.4,0}rcv}_{\top}(c_{2})\cdot\mathtt{\color[rgb]{0,0.4,0}rcv}_{\top}(c_{3})\cdot\mathtt{\color[rgb]{0,0.4,0}rcv}_{\bot}(c_{2})\cdot\mathtt{\color[rgb]{0,0.4,0}rcv}_{\bot}(c_{3})\cdot\mathtt{\color[rgb]{0,0.4,0}rcv}_{\bot}(c_{1})

If
\gamma_{2}
= true and
\gamma_{3}
= false, then

C_{j}=\mathtt{\color[rgb]{0,0.4,0}rcv}_{\top}(c_{1})\cdot\mathtt{\color[rgb]{0,0.4,0}rcv}_{\top}(c_{2})\cdot\mathtt{\color[rgb]{0,0.4,0}rcv}_{\bot}(c_{2})\cdot\mathtt{\color[rgb]{0,0.4,0}rcv}_{\bot}(c_{3})\cdot\mathtt{\color[rgb]{0,0.4,0}rcv}_{\top}(c_{3})\cdot\mathtt{\color[rgb]{0,0.4,0}rcv}_{\bot}(c_{1})

If
\gamma_{2}
= false and
\gamma_{3}
= true, then

C_{j}=\mathtt{\color[rgb]{0,0.4,0}rcv}_{\top}(c_{1})\cdot\mathtt{\color[rgb]{0,0.4,0}rcv}_{\bot}(c_{2})\cdot\mathtt{\color[rgb]{0,0.4,0}rcv}_{\top}(c_{2})\cdot\mathtt{\color[rgb]{0,0.4,0}rcv}_{\top}(c_{3})\cdot\mathtt{\color[rgb]{0,0.4,0}rcv}_{\bot}(c_{3})\cdot\mathtt{\color[rgb]{0,0.4,0}rcv}_{\bot}(c_{1})

If
\gamma_{2}
= false and
\gamma_{3}
= false, then

C_{j}=\mathtt{\color[rgb]{0,0.4,0}rcv}_{\top}(c_{1})\cdot\mathtt{\color[rgb]{0,0.4,0}rcv}_{\bot}(c_{2})\cdot\mathtt{\color[rgb]{0,0.4,0}rcv}_{\top}(c_{2})\cdot\mathtt{\color[rgb]{0,0.4,0}rcv}_{\bot}(c_{3})\cdot\mathtt{\color[rgb]{0,0.4,0}rcv}_{\top}(c_{3})\cdot\mathtt{\color[rgb]{0,0.4,0}rcv}_{\bot}(c_{1})

Firstly, it’s easy to verify these linear sequences respect program order.
To argue that they also satisfy the reads-from relation,
we have the following observations.

•

If
x_{l}
is assigned to be true, then
\mathtt{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.8}snd}^{l}_{\top}(\mathtt{ch}_{1})<^{\sigma}_{\mathsf{\textsf{tr}}}\mathtt{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.8}snd}^{l}_{\bot}(\mathtt{ch}_{1})
and
\mathtt{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.8}snd}^{l}_{\top}(\mathtt{ch}_{2})<^{\sigma}_{\mathsf{\textsf{tr}}}\mathtt{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.8}snd}^{l}_{\bot}(\mathtt{ch}_{2})
in
A_{i,l}
for any
l,i
.

•

If
x_{l}
is assigned to be false, then
\mathtt{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.8}snd}^{l}_{\bot}(\mathtt{ch}_{1})<^{\sigma}_{\mathsf{\textsf{tr}}}\mathtt{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.8}snd}^{l}_{\top}(\mathtt{ch}_{1})
and
\mathtt{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.8}snd}^{l}_{\bot}(\mathtt{ch}_{2})<^{\sigma}_{\mathsf{\textsf{tr}}}\mathtt{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.8}snd}^{l}_{\top}(\mathtt{ch}_{2})
in
A_{i,l}
for any
l,i
.

•

The way we assign reads-from relation for
\mathtt{\color[rgb]{0,0.4,0}rcv}_{\top}(c_{q}),\mathtt{\color[rgb]{0,0.4,0}rcv}_{\bot}(c_{q})
matches the order we send to
c_{q}
in
A_{j}

and in
A_{j}
,

\mathtt{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.8}snd}_{\bot}(c_{q})<^{\sigma}_{\mathsf{\textsf{tr}}}\mathtt{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.8}snd}_{\top}(c_{q})
iff
x_{j_{q}}
= false.

Therefore, the reads-from relation is also satisfied.
This proves
\sigma
is indeed a valid concretization and thus the
VCh-rf
problem is consistent.

∎

C.4.
Proof for Section
4.4

See
4.3

Proof.

In the following proofs, we refer to the lexicographical order of pairs of indices
\langle i,j\rangle
. A pair
\langle i_{1},j_{1}\rangle
is lexicographically before
\langle i_{2},j_{2}\rangle
if
i_{1}<i_{2}
, or, in the case where
i_{1}=i_{2}
, if
j_{1}<j_{2}
. To say that
\langle i_{1},j_{1}\rangle
is lexicographically before
\langle i_{2},j_{2}\rangle
, we write
\langle i_{1},j_{1}\rangle<_{\textit{lex}}\langle i_{2},j_{2}\rangle
, and we use
\leq_{\textit{lex}}
as the reflexive closure of
<_{\textit{lex}}
. This can be extended to pairs of vectors
\langle a_{i},b_{j}\rangle\in A\times B
, referring to the indices of the vectors.

Proof of correctness (Orthogonal pair
\Rightarrow
Consistency).

We first prove that if an orthogonal pair
a_{i}\in A,b_{j}\in B
exists, the resulting total execution
{\mathcal{X}}=\langle\mathsf{S},{\textsf{po}},{\textsf{rf}}\rangle
is consistent. Let
\langle a_{i_{1}},b_{j_{1}}\rangle
be the lexicographically earliest pair of orthogonal vectors, and let
\langle a_{i_{2}},b_{j_{2}}\rangle
be the lexicographically last pair of orthogonal vectors. We now define a partial order
<_{\textit{sat}}
on the events of the total execution based on these vectors. Afterwards, we show that there exists a linearization of
<_{\textit{sat}}
that is a well-formed execution.
It is defined by (the transitive closure of):

(1)

e_{1}<_{\textit{sat}}e_{2}
for all
e_{1},e_{2}
where
\langle e_{1},e_{2}\rangle\in({\textsf{po}}\cup{\textsf{rf}})^{+}
.

(2)

\mathtt{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.8}snd}_{a_{i}}(\alpha)<_{\textit{sat}}\mathtt{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.8}snd}_{b_{j}}(\alpha)
for all
\langle a_{i},b_{j}\rangle\in A\times B
, where
\langle i,j\rangle\leq_{\textit{lex}}\langle i_{1},j_{1}\rangle
.

(3)

\mathtt{\color[rgb]{0,0.4,0}rcv}_{a_{i}}(\mathtt{ch}_{k})<_{\textit{sat}}\mathtt{\color[rgb]{0,0.4,0}rcv}_{b_{j}}(\mathtt{ch}_{k})
for all
\langle a_{i},b_{j}\rangle\in A\times B
and
1\leq k\leq d
, where both events exist and
\langle i,j\rangle<_{\textit{lex}}\langle i_{1},j_{1}\rangle
.

(4)

\mathtt{\color[rgb]{0,0.4,0}rcv}_{a_{i}}(\beta)<_{\textit{sat}}\mathtt{\color[rgb]{0,0.4,0}rcv}_{b}(\beta)
for all
i<i_{1}
.

(5)

\mathtt{\color[rgb]{0,0.4,0}rcv}_{b_{j}}(\alpha)<_{\textit{sat}}\mathtt{\color[rgb]{0,0.4,0}rcv}_{a_{i}}(\alpha)
for all
\langle a_{i},b_{j}\rangle\in A\times B
, where
\langle i_{2},j_{2}\rangle<_{\textit{lex}}\langle i,j\rangle
.

(6)

\mathtt{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.8}snd}_{b_{j}}(\mathtt{ch}_{k})<_{\textit{sat}}\mathtt{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.8}snd}_{a_{i}}(\mathtt{ch}_{k})
for all
\langle a_{i},b_{j}\rangle\in A\times B
and
1\leq k\leq d
, where both events exist and
\langle i_{2},j_{2}\rangle<_{\textit{lex}}\langle i,j\rangle
.

(7)

\mathtt{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.8}snd}_{b}(\beta)<_{\textit{sat}}\mathtt{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.8}snd}_{a_{i}}(\beta)
for all
i\geq i_{2}
.

To verify that this is indeed a (strict) partial order, we need to show that it is asymmetric, or, if we think of the execution as a graph, acyclic. To show this, we first show that if there is a cycle, then there is a cycle of the following form: First a
po
step in
\tau_{1}
, then a step from rules (2)-(4), followed by a
po
step in
\tau_{2}
, and finally a step from (5)-(7).

It is trivial from the construction that (1) itself does not create a cycle, so a rule from (2)-(7) is needed, but these are all edges between
\tau_{1}
and
\tau_{2}
. Furthermore, a
{\textsf{rf}}\setminus{\textsf{po}}
edge cannot be part of the cycle for the following reason: The only two cross-thread
rf
edges are on the
\gamma
and
\delta
channels. Examining the read of
\gamma
, the only edge from
\tau_{2}
to
\tau_{1}
that could form a cycle based on this read would have to go from
\mathtt{\color[rgb]{0,0.4,0}rcv}_{b_{1}}(\alpha)
to
\mathtt{\color[rgb]{0,0.4,0}rcv}_{a_{1}}(\alpha)
, since only rule (5) could apply. But this is impossible, since
\langle a_{1},b_{1}\rangle
is the lexicographically first pair. Next, the read of
\delta
fails for a similar reason: The only possible cycle caused by this read would be from
\mathtt{\color[rgb]{0,0.4,0}rcv}_{a_{n}}(\mathtt{ch}_{k})
to
\mathtt{\color[rgb]{0,0.4,0}rcv}_{b_{n}}(\mathtt{ch}_{k})
for some
k
, but only rule (3) could apply, which is impossible, since
\langle a_{n},b_{n}\rangle
is lexicographically last.
Therefore, we need a non-
rf
edge from
\tau_{1}
to
\tau_{2}
and a non-
rf
edge from
\tau_{2}
to
\tau_{1}
, which, by inspection, can only come from (2)-(4) and (5)-(7), respectively. We can notice that no edges go both ways on the same combination of event type (read or write) and channel, so we need a
po
step on both threads. Finally, if there is a cycle with more than four events, it is easy to convince oneself that it is possible to find a subset of four of those events that also form a cycle.

We can now look at all combinations of rules between (2)-(4) and (5)-(7) to show that none of them can cause a cycle. Two of the cases are not possible due to the source event kind (event type and channel) of the (2)-(4) edge only ever appearing before the destination event kind of the (5)-(7) edge in the construction. This is the case for (2) and (5) as well as (2) and (7). Similarly, sometimes the destination event kind of the (2)-(4) edge only appears after the source event kind of the (5)-(7) edge. These pairs are (3) and (6), (4) and (6), and (4) and (7). We look at the remaining pairs below:

(2) and (6).:

We must have
\mathtt{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.8}snd}_{a_{i}}(\alpha)<_{\textit{sat}}\mathtt{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.8}snd}_{b_{j}}(\alpha)
and
\mathtt{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.8}snd}_{b_{j^{\prime}}}(\mathtt{ch}_{k})<_{\textit{sat}}\mathtt{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.8}snd}_{a_{i^{\prime}}}(\mathtt{ch}_{k})
for some
i
,
i^{\prime}
,
j
,
j^{\prime}
, and
k
. Due to the
po
ordering, we have
i^{\prime}\leq i
and
j^{\prime}\leq j
. This contradicts
\langle i,j\rangle\leq_{\textit{lex}}\langle i_{1},j_{1}\rangle\leq_{\textit{lex}}\langle i_{2},j_{2}\rangle<_{\textit{lex}}\langle i^{\prime},j^{\prime}\rangle
.

(3) and (5).:

We have
\mathtt{\color[rgb]{0,0.4,0}rcv}_{a_{i}}(\mathtt{ch}_{k})<_{\textit{sat}}\mathtt{\color[rgb]{0,0.4,0}rcv}_{b_{j}}(\mathtt{ch}_{k})
and
\mathtt{\color[rgb]{0,0.4,0}rcv}_{b_{j^{\prime}}}(\alpha)<_{\textit{sat}}\mathtt{\color[rgb]{0,0.4,0}rcv}_{a_{i^{\prime}}}(\alpha)
for some
i
,
i^{\prime}
,
j
,
j^{\prime}
, and
k
, where
i^{\prime}\leq i
and
j^{\prime}\leq j+1
by the
po
ordering. This contradicts
\langle i,j\rangle<_{\textit{lex}}\langle i_{1},j_{1}\rangle\leq_{\textit{lex}}\langle i_{2},j_{2}\rangle<_{\textit{lex}}\langle i^{\prime},j^{\prime}\rangle
, since
\langle i^{\prime},j^{\prime}\rangle
can at most be
\langle i,j+1\rangle
, but there has to be a pair between
\langle i,j\rangle
and
\langle i^{\prime},j^{\prime}\rangle
.

(3) and (7).:

We have
\mathtt{\color[rgb]{0,0.4,0}rcv}_{a_{i}}(\mathtt{ch}_{k})<_{\textit{sat}}\mathtt{\color[rgb]{0,0.4,0}rcv}_{b_{j}}(\mathtt{ch}_{k})
and
\mathtt{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.8}snd}_{b}(\beta)<_{\textit{sat}}\mathtt{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.8}snd}_{a_{i^{\prime}}}(\beta)
for
j=n
and some
i
,
i^{\prime}
, and
k
, where
i_{2}\leq i^{\prime}\leq i
by
po
. This contradicts
\langle i,n\rangle=\langle i,j\rangle<_{\textit{lex}}\langle i_{1},j_{1}\rangle
.

(4) and (5).:

Finally, we have
\mathtt{\color[rgb]{0,0.4,0}rcv}_{a_{i}}(\beta)<_{\textit{sat}}\mathtt{\color[rgb]{0,0.4,0}rcv}_{b}(\beta)
and
\mathtt{\color[rgb]{0,0.4,0}rcv}_{b_{j}}(\alpha)<_{\textit{sat}}\mathtt{\color[rgb]{0,0.4,0}rcv}_{a_{i^{\prime}}}(\alpha)
for some
i
,
i^{\prime}
,
j
, and
k
, where
i^{\prime}-1\leq i<i_{1}
by
po
, and thereby
i^{\prime}\leq i_{1}
. This contradicts
\langle i_{2},j_{2}\rangle<_{\textit{lex}}\langle i^{\prime},j\rangle
, unless
i^{\prime}=i_{1}=i_{2}
and
j>j_{2}
. But the
po
ordering of
\mathtt{\color[rgb]{0,0.4,0}rcv}_{b}(\beta)
before
\mathtt{\color[rgb]{0,0.4,0}rcv}_{b_{j}}(\alpha)
means that
j=1
, and thus,
j\leq j_{2}
.

With this, we have shown that
<_{\textit{sat}}
is indeed a partial order. What remains is to show that there is a linearization
\sigma
of
<_{\textit{sat}}
(respecting
{\mathcal{X}}
) that is a well-formed execution.
To do so, we first show that
<_{\textit{sat}}
is
saturated
, i.e.
\mathtt{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.8}snd}_{1}(\mathtt{ch})<_{\textit{sat}}\mathtt{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.8}snd}_{2}(\mathtt{ch})
iff
\mathtt{\color[rgb]{0,0.4,0}rcv}_{1}(\mathtt{ch})<_{\textit{sat}}\mathtt{\color[rgb]{0,0.4,0}rcv}_{2}(\mathtt{ch})
. The method will be to look at each rule in turn and verify the property for all events ordered by this rule.
Note that the property is easy to verify if the two events are on the same thread, since, except for
\gamma
and
\delta
(which are trivial), all reads are on the same channels as the writes.
Therefore, we look at rule (
i
), ordering
e_{1}<_{\textit{sat}}e_{2}
across both threads, and consider all pairs of events where the first is
po
before
e_{1}
(including
e_{1}
) and the second is
po
after
e_{2}
(including
e_{2}
).
The reason we do not have to consider e.g. other events
(<_{\textit{sat}}\setminus\ {\textsf{po}})
-before
e_{1}
is that such events on the same thread as
e_{2}
, and vice versa for events after
e_{2}
.

(1)

Per the reasoning above, it is sufficient to consider
\langle e_{1},e_{2}\rangle\in({\textsf{rf}}\setminus{\textsf{po}})
. Considering
(\mathtt{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.8}snd}(\gamma)<_{\textit{sat}}\mathtt{\color[rgb]{0,0.4,0}rcv}(\gamma))
first, we can see that
\mathtt{\color[rgb]{0,0.4,0}rcv}_{a_{1}}(\alpha)
and
\mathtt{\color[rgb]{0,0.4,0}rcv}_{b_{1}}(\alpha)
are ordered, so we have to show that
\mathtt{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.8}snd}_{a_{1}}(\alpha)<_{\textit{sat}}\mathtt{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.8}snd}_{b_{1}}(\alpha)
. This follows immediately from rule (2). Next, we consider
(\mathtt{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.8}snd}(\delta)<_{\textit{sat}}\mathtt{\color[rgb]{0,0.4,0}rcv}(\delta))
, which orders
\mathtt{\color[rgb]{0,0.4,0}rcv}_{b_{n}}(\mathtt{ch}_{k})
before
\mathtt{\color[rgb]{0,0.4,0}rcv}_{a_{n}}(\mathtt{ch}_{k})
for all
k
where both events exist. If there is such a
k
,
a_{n}
and
b_{n}
must not be orthogonal. Thus,
\langle i_{2},j_{2}\rangle<_{\textit{lex}}\langle n,n\rangle
, and the rest follows from rule (6).

(2)

\mathtt{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.8}snd}_{a_{i}}(\alpha)<_{\textit{sat}}\mathtt{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.8}snd}_{b_{j}}(\alpha)
: We have to show
\mathtt{\color[rgb]{0,0.4,0}rcv}_{a_{i}}(\alpha)<_{\textit{sat}}\mathtt{\color[rgb]{0,0.4,0}rcv}_{b_{j}}(\alpha)
. We look at three cases for the value of
\langle i,j\rangle
:
\langle 1,1\rangle
,
\langle i^{\prime},1\rangle
, and
\langle i,j^{\prime}\rangle
, where
i^{\prime}\neq 1
and
j^{\prime}\neq 1
.

\langle 1,1\rangle

Follows immediately from the read of
\gamma
.

\langle i^{\prime},1\rangle

It follows from
\langle i^{\prime},1\rangle\leq_{\textit{lex}}\langle i_{1},j_{1}\rangle
that
i^{\prime}-1<i_{1}
. The ordering then follows transitively from rule (4).

\langle i,j^{\prime}\rangle

From
\langle i,j^{\prime}\rangle\leq_{\textit{lex}}\langle i_{1},j_{1}\rangle
we get that
\langle i,j^{\prime}-1\rangle<_{\textit{lex}}\langle i_{1},j_{1}\rangle
, which, by transitivity, gives the correct ordering from rule (3).

We also have to consider anything
po
-before
\mathtt{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.8}snd}_{a_{i}}(\alpha)
against anything
po
-after
\mathtt{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.8}snd}_{b_{j}}(\alpha)
. This may include other writes to
\alpha
, but these cases are already covered transitively by what we have shown. For writes to
\mathtt{ch}_{k}
we still need to show that
\mathtt{\color[rgb]{0,0.4,0}rcv}_{a_{i}}(\mathtt{ch}_{k})<_{\textit{sat}}\mathtt{\color[rgb]{0,0.4,0}rcv}_{b_{j}}(\mathtt{ch}_{k})
(other such writes less than
\langle i,j\rangle
are covered transitively). The fact that
\mathtt{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.8}snd}_{a_{i}}(\mathtt{ch}_{k})
and
\mathtt{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.8}snd}_{b_{j}}(\mathtt{ch}_{k})
both exist means that
a_{i}
and
b_{j}
are not orthogonal, which means that
\langle i_{1},j_{1}\rangle\neq\langle i,j\rangle
. Together with
\langle i,j\rangle\leq_{\textit{lex}}\langle i_{1},j_{1}\rangle
, this means that rule (3) applies, and we are done.

(3)

\mathtt{\color[rgb]{0,0.4,0}rcv}_{a_{i}}(\mathtt{ch}_{k})<_{\textit{sat}}\mathtt{\color[rgb]{0,0.4,0}rcv}_{b_{j}}(\mathtt{ch}_{k})
: We must show
\mathtt{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.8}snd}_{a_{i}}(\mathtt{ch}_{k})<_{\textit{sat}}\mathtt{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.8}snd}_{b_{j}}(\mathtt{ch}_{k})
. Rule (2) can be applied immediately, which transitively gives the right ordering.
Reads of
\alpha
and reads/writes for
\beta
could also be ordered by this rule. For
\alpha
, we have to show
\mathtt{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.8}snd}_{a_{i}}(\alpha)<_{\textit{sat}}\mathtt{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.8}snd}_{b_{j+1}}(\alpha)
(for
j<n
). This follows from rule (2), since
\langle i,j\rangle<_{\textit{lex}}\langle i_{1},j_{1}\rangle
, so
\langle i,j+1\rangle\leq_{\textit{lex}}\langle i_{1},j_{1}\rangle
.
For reads of
\beta
we have to show
\mathtt{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.8}snd}_{a_{i-1}}(\beta)<_{\textit{sat}}\mathtt{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.8}snd}_{b}(\beta)
(for
i>1
). It holds that
\langle i-1,n\rangle<_{\textit{lex}}\langle i,j\rangle<_{\textit{lex}}\langle i_{1},j_{1}\rangle
, so we can apply rule (3) to get
\mathtt{\color[rgb]{0,0.4,0}rcv}_{a_{i-1}}(\mathtt{ch}_{k})<_{\textit{sat}}\mathtt{\color[rgb]{0,0.4,0}rcv}_{b_{n}}(\mathtt{ch}_{k})
, from which the ordering follows transitively. Finally, for writes to
\beta
(where
i>1
and
j=n
), we have to show
\mathtt{\color[rgb]{0,0.4,0}rcv}_{a_{i}}(\beta)<_{\textit{sat}}\mathtt{\color[rgb]{0,0.4,0}rcv}_{b}(\beta)
.
\langle i,n\rangle=\langle i,j\rangle<_{\textit{lex}}\langle i_{1},j_{1}\rangle
implies that
i<i_{1}
, so rule (4) gives us the ordering.

(4)

\mathtt{\color[rgb]{0,0.4,0}rcv}_{a_{i}}(\beta)<_{\textit{sat}}\mathtt{\color[rgb]{0,0.4,0}rcv}_{b}(\beta)
: We have to show
\mathtt{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.8}snd}_{a_{i}}(\beta)<_{\textit{sat}}\mathtt{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.8}snd}_{b}(\beta)
. From
i<i_{1}
we get
\langle i,n\rangle<_{\textit{lex}}\langle i_{1},i_{2}\rangle
, so the ordering follows transitively from (3). This also transitively orders
\mathtt{\color[rgb]{0,0.4,0}rcv}_{a_{i+1}}(\alpha)
with
\mathtt{\color[rgb]{0,0.4,0}rcv}_{b_{1}}(\alpha)
(for
i<n
), so we show
\mathtt{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.8}snd}_{a_{i+1}}(\alpha)<_{\textit{sat}}\mathtt{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.8}snd}_{b_{1}}(\alpha)
. Since
i+1\leq i_{1}
,
\langle i+1,1\rangle\leq_{\textit{lex}}\langle i_{1},j_{1}\rangle
, which means that rule (2) applies.

(5)

\mathtt{\color[rgb]{0,0.4,0}rcv}_{b_{j}}(\alpha)<_{\textit{sat}}\mathtt{\color[rgb]{0,0.4,0}rcv}_{a_{i}}(\alpha)
: We must show
\mathtt{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.8}snd}_{b_{j}}(\alpha)<_{\textit{sat}}\mathtt{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.8}snd}_{a_{i}}(\alpha)
. Since
\langle i_{2},j_{2}\rangle<_{\textit{lex}}\langle i,j\rangle
,
a_{i}
and
b_{j}
are not orthogonal, which means that
\mathtt{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.8}snd}_{b_{j}}(\mathtt{ch}_{k})
and
\mathtt{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.8}snd}_{a_{i}}(\mathtt{ch}_{k})
exist for some
k
. Furthermore, these are ordered by rule (6), which transitively orders the
\alpha
writes.
Orderings between reads of
\alpha
can transitively order reads of
\mathtt{ch}_{k}
(for some
k
) as well as reads/writes for
\beta
.
For
\mathtt{ch}_{k}
, we must show
\mathtt{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.8}snd}_{b_{j-1}}(\mathtt{ch}_{k})<_{\textit{sat}}\mathtt{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.8}snd}_{a_{i}}(\mathtt{ch}_{k})
(for
j>1
). Since
a_{i}
and
b_{j-1}
have reads to
k
in common, they are not orthogonal. From this, along with
\langle i,j-1\rangle
being the pair just before
\langle i,j\rangle
, we get
\langle i_{2},j_{2}\rangle<_{\textit{lex}}\langle i,j-1\rangle
, which means rule (6) applies.
Looking now at
\mathtt{\color[rgb]{0,0.4,0}rcv}_{b}(\beta)<_{\textit{sat}}\mathtt{\color[rgb]{0,0.4,0}rcv}_{a_{i-1}}(\beta)
(for
j=1
and
i>1
), the ordering on writes follows from rule (7), since
\langle i_{2},j_{2}\rangle<_{\textit{lex}}\langle i,1\rangle
and, hence,
i-1\geq i_{2}
.
Finally, writes to
\beta
can also be ordered, so we must show
\mathtt{\color[rgb]{0,0.4,0}rcv}_{b}(\beta)<_{\textit{sat}}\mathtt{\color[rgb]{0,0.4,0}rcv}_{a_{i}}(\beta)
(for
i<n
). We instead show
\mathtt{\color[rgb]{0,0.4,0}rcv}_{b_{1}}(\alpha)<_{\textit{sat}}\mathtt{\color[rgb]{0,0.4,0}rcv}_{a_{i+1}}(\alpha)
, from which the ordering follows transitively. This follows by rule (5), since
\langle i_{2},j_{2}\rangle<_{\textit{lex}}\langle i,j\rangle<_{\textit{lex}}\langle i+1,1\rangle
.

(6)

\mathtt{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.8}snd}_{b_{j}}(\mathtt{ch}_{k})<_{\textit{sat}}\mathtt{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.8}snd}_{a_{i}}(\mathtt{ch}_{k})
: We first show
\mathtt{\color[rgb]{0,0.4,0}rcv}_{b_{j}}(\mathtt{ch}_{k})<_{\textit{sat}}\mathtt{\color[rgb]{0,0.4,0}rcv}_{a_{i}}(\mathtt{ch}_{k})
. There are three cases for
\langle i,j\rangle
:
\langle n,n\rangle
,
\langle i^{\prime},n\rangle
and
\langle i,j^{\prime}\rangle
, where
i^{\prime}\neq n
and
j^{\prime}\neq n
.

\langle n,n\rangle

Follows immediately from the read of
\delta
.

\langle i^{\prime},n\rangle

It follows from
\langle i_{2},j_{2}\rangle<_{\textit{lex}}\langle i^{\prime},n\rangle
that
i^{\prime}\geq i_{2}
. From here, we apply rule (7) and get the ordering transitively.

\langle i,j^{\prime}\rangle

We have
\langle i_{2},j_{2}\rangle<_{\textit{lex}}\langle i,j^{\prime}\rangle<_{\textit{lex}}\langle i,j^{\prime}+1\rangle
, which means rule (5) applies, transitively ordering the reads to
\mathtt{ch}_{k}
.

The ordering of writes to
\mathtt{ch}_{k}
may also order writes to
\alpha
, but
\mathtt{\color[rgb]{0,0.4,0}rcv}_{b_{j}}(\alpha)<_{\textit{sat}}\mathtt{\color[rgb]{0,0.4,0}rcv}_{a_{i}}(\alpha)
follows immediately from rule (5).

(7)

\mathtt{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.8}snd}_{b}(\beta)<_{\textit{sat}}\mathtt{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.8}snd}_{a_{i}}(\beta)
: We must show that
\mathtt{\color[rgb]{0,0.4,0}rcv}_{b}(\beta)<_{\textit{sat}}\mathtt{\color[rgb]{0,0.4,0}rcv}_{a_{i}}(\beta)
, which we do by showing that
\mathtt{\color[rgb]{0,0.4,0}rcv}_{b_{1}}(\alpha)<_{\textit{sat}}\mathtt{\color[rgb]{0,0.4,0}rcv}_{a_{i+1}}(\alpha)
.
From
i\geq i_{2}
it follows that
\langle i_{2},j_{2}\rangle<_{\textit{lex}}\langle i+1,1\rangle
, which means that rule (5) can be applied to get the desired ordering. A read
\mathtt{\color[rgb]{0,0.4,0}rcv}_{b_{n}}(\mathtt{ch}_{k})
could be ordered before another read
\mathtt{\color[rgb]{0,0.4,0}rcv}_{a_{i}}(\mathtt{ch}_{k})
, so we show that
\mathtt{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.8}snd}_{b_{n}}(\mathtt{ch}_{k})<_{\textit{sat}}\mathtt{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.8}snd}_{a_{i}}(\mathtt{ch}_{k})
.
It follows from
i\geq i_{2}
that
\langle i_{2},j_{2}\rangle\leq_{\textit{lex}}\langle i,n\rangle
. But the existence of
\mathtt{\color[rgb]{0,0.4,0}rcv}_{b_{n}}(\mathtt{ch}_{k})
and
\mathtt{\color[rgb]{0,0.4,0}rcv}_{a_{i}}(\mathtt{ch}_{k})
means that
a_{i}
and
b_{n}
are not orthogonal, so
\langle i_{2},j_{2}\rangle<_{\textit{lex}}\langle i,n\rangle
, and rule (6) applies.

Define
\sigma
as follows: Given two events
e_{1},e_{2}\in S
, order
e_{1}
before
e_{2}
if
e_{1}<_{\textit{sat}}e_{2}
and vice versa, otherwise order the event from
\tau_{1}
first (if they are on the same thread, they are ordered by
<_{\textit{sat}}
). This is a total order because it is the order you get by greedily picking events from
\tau_{1}
as long as no unpicked event from
\tau_{2}
is
<_{\textit{sat}}
-before.

We have to show that for each channel
\mathtt{ch}\in\mathsf{Channels(\sigma)}
the
i
-th read of
{\sigma}{\downharpoonright}_{\mathtt{ch}}
reads the
i
-th write. To do so, we show the equivalent property that, if
\mathtt{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.8}snd}_{1}(\mathtt{ch})<^{\sigma}_{\mathsf{\textsf{tr}}}\mathtt{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.8}snd}_{2}(\mathtt{ch})
, then
\mathtt{\color[rgb]{0,0.4,0}rcv}_{1}(\mathtt{ch})<^{\sigma}_{\mathsf{\textsf{tr}}}\mathtt{\color[rgb]{0,0.4,0}rcv}_{2}(\mathtt{ch})
.
Let
\mathtt{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.8}snd}_{1},\mathtt{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.8}snd}_{2}\in{\sigma}{\downharpoonright}_{\mathtt{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.8}snd}(\mathtt{ch})}
be two writes such that
\mathtt{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.8}snd}_{1}<^{\sigma}_{\mathsf{\textsf{tr}}}\mathtt{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.8}snd}_{2}
. If
\mathtt{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.8}snd}_{1}<_{\textit{sat}}\mathtt{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.8}snd}_{2}
, then
\mathtt{\color[rgb]{0,0.4,0}rcv}_{1}<_{\textit{sat}}\mathtt{\color[rgb]{0,0.4,0}rcv}_{2}
and thus
\mathtt{\color[rgb]{0,0.4,0}rcv}_{1}<^{\sigma}_{\mathsf{\textsf{tr}}}\mathtt{\color[rgb]{0,0.4,0}rcv}_{2}
, since
<_{\textit{sat}}
is saturated.
If
\mathtt{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.8}snd}_{1}\not<_{\textit{sat}}\mathtt{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.8}snd}_{2}
, assume for contradiction that
\mathtt{\color[rgb]{0,0.4,0}rcv}_{1}
and
\mathtt{\color[rgb]{0,0.4,0}rcv}_{2}
are ordered by
<_{\textit{sat}}
. Either ordering (
\mathtt{\color[rgb]{0,0.4,0}rcv}_{1}<_{\textit{sat}}\mathtt{\color[rgb]{0,0.4,0}rcv}_{2}
or
\mathtt{\color[rgb]{0,0.4,0}rcv}_{2}<_{\textit{sat}}\mathtt{\color[rgb]{0,0.4,0}rcv}_{1}
) would imply that
\mathtt{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.8}snd}_{1}
and
\mathtt{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.8}snd}_{2}
are also ordered, since
<_{\textit{sat}}
is saturated.
In the case of
\mathtt{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.8}snd}_{1}<_{\textit{sat}}\mathtt{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.8}snd}_{2}
this directly contradicts the premise, and
\mathtt{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.8}snd}_{2}<_{\textit{sat}}\mathtt{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.8}snd}_{1}
contradicts
\mathtt{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.8}snd}_{1}<^{\sigma}_{\mathsf{\textsf{tr}}}\mathtt{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.8}snd}_{2}
. Hence, both the reads and the writes are unordered by
<_{\textit{sat}}
.
In all channels except
\gamma
and
\delta
the reads are on the same threads as the corresponding writes, thus they will be ordered the same in
\sigma
. Lastly, the
\gamma
and
\delta
channels only have one write each, so the property is trivial for these.

Proof of correctness (Consistency
\Rightarrow
Orthogonal pair).

Next, we prove that if the total execution is consistent, there is an orthogonal pair. To show this, we prove the contra-positive statement: If there are no orthogonal pairs, the total execution is not consistent. More specifically, we will show that the lack of an orthogonal pair leads to the derivation of a cyclic ordering between
\mathtt{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.8}snd}_{a_{n}}(\alpha)
and
\mathtt{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.8}snd}_{b_{n}}(\alpha)
by saturation.

To show one direction, we prove by induction that
\mathtt{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.8}snd}_{a_{i}}(\alpha)
is ordered before
\mathtt{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.8}snd}_{b_{j}}(\alpha)
for all
i
and
j
less than
n
. The induction is in the lexicographical order of
\langle i,j\rangle
, i.e.
\langle 1,1\rangle
is the base case, and the next element after
\langle i,j\rangle
is either
\langle i,j+1\rangle
if
j\neq n
or
\langle i+1,1\rangle
if
j=n
.

•

Base Case:
Initially,
\mathtt{\color[rgb]{0,0.4,0}rcv}_{a_{1}}(\alpha)
is ordered before
\mathtt{\color[rgb]{0,0.4,0}rcv}_{b_{1}}(\alpha)
through the read of
\gamma
, which orders
\mathtt{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.8}snd}_{a_{1}}(\alpha)
before
\mathtt{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.8}snd}_{b_{1}}(\alpha)
by saturation.

•

Induction:
We prove that
\mathtt{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.8}snd}_{a_{i}}(\alpha)
is ordered before
\mathtt{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.8}snd}_{b_{j}}(\alpha)
(for
\langle i,j\rangle\neq\langle 1,1\rangle
). We do case distinction on (1) if
j=1
or (2) if
j\neq 1
.

(1)

If
j=1
, the induction hypothesis states that
\mathtt{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.8}snd}_{a_{i-1}}(\alpha)
is ordered before
\mathtt{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.8}snd}_{b_{n}}(\alpha)
. Since
a_{i-1}
and
b_{n}
are not orthogonal, they must both have a 1 in some dimension
k
, and there are therefore writes
\mathtt{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.8}snd}_{a_{i-1}}(\mathtt{ch}_{k})
and
\mathtt{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.8}snd}_{b_{n}}(\mathtt{ch}_{k})
.
By the induction hypothesis, these writes are ordered, which, by saturation, orders
\mathtt{\color[rgb]{0,0.4,0}rcv}_{a_{i-1}}(\mathtt{ch}_{k})
before
\mathtt{\color[rgb]{0,0.4,0}rcv}_{b_{n}}(\mathtt{ch}_{k})
.
This orders
\mathtt{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.8}snd}_{a_{i-1}}(\beta)
before
\mathtt{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.8}snd}_{b}(\beta)
. Applying saturation, the reads of
\alpha
for
a_{i}
and
b_{1}
are thus ordered.
By a final application of saturation,
\mathtt{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.8}snd}_{a_{i}}(\alpha)
is thus ordered before
\mathtt{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.8}snd}_{b_{1}}(\alpha)
.

(2)

In the case of
j\neq 1
, the induction hypothesis states that
\mathtt{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.8}snd}_{a_{i}}(\alpha)
is ordered before
\mathtt{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.8}snd}_{b_{j-1}}(\alpha)
. As before,
a_{i}
and
b_{j-1}
are not orthogonal, so for some
k
,
\mathtt{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.8}snd}_{a_{i}}(\mathtt{ch}_{k})
and
\mathtt{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.8}snd}_{b_{j-1}}(\mathtt{ch}_{k})
exist and are ordered.
By saturation,
\mathtt{\color[rgb]{0,0.4,0}rcv}_{a_{i}}(\mathtt{ch}_{k})
is ordered before
\mathtt{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.8}snd}_{b_{j-1}}(\mathtt{ch}_{k})
, which orders
\mathtt{\color[rgb]{0,0.4,0}rcv}_{a_{i}}(\alpha)
before
\mathtt{\color[rgb]{0,0.4,0}rcv}_{b_{j}}(\alpha)
. A final application of saturation then gives the desired ordering.

The last thing to show is that
\mathtt{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.8}snd}_{b_{n}}(\alpha)
is ordered before
\mathtt{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.8}snd}_{a_{n}}(\alpha)
. The read of
\delta
orders
\mathtt{\color[rgb]{0,0.4,0}rcv}_{b_{n}}(\mathtt{ch}_{k})
before
\mathtt{\color[rgb]{0,0.4,0}rcv}_{a_{n}}(\mathtt{ch}_{k})
for some
k
(both of which exist, since
a_{n}
and
b_{n}
are not orthogonal). The proof is then concluded by an application of saturation.

∎

Appendix D
Details in evaluation

D.1.
\mathsf{SMT}
encodings

Given a
VCh-rf
input
\langle\mathcal{X},\mathsf{cap},{\textsf{rf}}\rangle
,
where
{\mathcal{X}}=\langle\mathsf{S},{\textsf{po}}\rangle
.
We encode a
\mathsf{SMT}
formula
\psi
, s.t.
\psi
is satisfiable iff
\mathcal{X}
is consistent.

We first discuss the variables in
\psi
.
For each event
e\in\mathsf{S}
, we allocate an integer variable
0\leq x_{e}\leq n-1
, where
n
is the total number of events in
\mathcal{X}
.

x_{e}
indicates the position of
e
in a possible concretization.
More over, for each channel
\mathtt{ch}
,
we allocate
2n+2
variables
y^{\mathtt{ch}}_{\mathtt{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.8}snd},i}
and
y^{\mathtt{ch}}_{\mathtt{\color[rgb]{0,0.4,0}rcv},i}
, where
0\leq i\leq n
.
Intuitively,
y^{\mathtt{ch}}_{\mathtt{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.8}snd},i}
and
y^{\mathtt{ch}}_{\mathtt{\color[rgb]{0,0.4,0}rcv},i}
stands for the total number of send / receive events to
\mathtt{ch}
at the prefix with
i
events of a possible concretization.

Now we discuss the content of
\psi
.
At a high level,
\psi
can be decomposed into several components.

\psi=\psi_{unique}\wedge\psi_{porf}\wedge\psi_{\textsf{FIFO}}\wedge\psi_{cap}

where
\psi_{unique}
ensures for all
e\in\mathsf{S}
,
x_{e}
is between
0
and
n-1
, and
x_{e}
is unique among all events (i.e. if
e\neq e^{\prime}
, then
x_{e}\neq x_{e^{\prime}}
).

\psi_{porf}
ensures program order and reads-from relation.

\psi_{\textsf{FIFO}}
ensures the
FIFO
property of channels.

\psi_{cap}
ensures capacity constraints of channels.

\psi_{unique}
is of the following form.

\psi_{unique}=(\bigwedge_{e\in\mathsf{S}}{0\leq x_{e}\leq n-1})\wedge(\bigwedge_{e,e^{\prime}\in\mathsf{S},\;e\neq e^{\prime}}{x_{e}\neq x_{e^{\prime}}})

Recall that we use
\mathsf{succ}(e)
to denote the immediate thread successor of
e
.

\psi_{porf}=\psi^{po}_{porf}\wedge\psi^{rf-sync}_{porf}\wedge\psi^{rf-async}_{porf}
, where

\psi^{po}_{porf}=\bigwedge_{e\in\mathsf{S},\;\mathsf{succ}(e)\neq\bot}{x_{e}<x_{\mathsf{succ}(e)}}

\psi^{rf\text{-}sync}_{porf}=\bigwedge_{(\mathtt{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.8}snd}(\mathtt{ch}),\mathtt{\color[rgb]{0,0.4,0}rcv}(\mathtt{ch})\in{\textsf{rf}},\mathsf{cap}(\mathtt{ch})=0}{x_{\mathtt{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.8}snd}(\mathtt{ch})}+1=x_{\mathtt{\color[rgb]{0,0.4,0}rcv}(\mathtt{ch})}}

\psi^{rf\text{-}async}_{porf}=\bigwedge_{(\mathtt{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.8}snd}(\mathtt{ch}),\mathtt{\color[rgb]{0,0.4,0}rcv}(\mathtt{ch})\in{\textsf{rf}},\mathsf{cap}(\mathtt{ch})>0}{x_{\mathtt{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.8}snd}(\mathtt{ch})}<x_{\mathtt{\color[rgb]{0,0.4,0}rcv}(\mathtt{ch})}}

\psi^{po}_{porf}
ensures program order.

\psi^{rf\text{-}sync}_{porf}
requires that for any synchronous channel
\mathtt{ch}
, all receive events should be immediately after its matching send event.

\psi^{rf\text{-}async}_{porf}
requires that for any asynchronous channel
\mathtt{ch}
, all receive events should be after its matching send event, but there can be some other events in between.

\psi_{\textsf{FIFO}}=\psi^{matched}_{\textsf{FIFO}}\wedge\psi^{unmatched}_{\textsf{FIFO}}
, where

\psi^{matched}_{\textsf{FIFO}}=\bigwedge_{\mathtt{ch}\in\mathcal{C}}{\bigwedge_{\scriptsize\begin{aligned} \begin{array}[]{c}(\mathtt{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.8}snd}_{1}(\mathtt{ch}),\mathtt{\color[rgb]{0,0.4,0}rcv}_{1}(\mathtt{ch}))\in{\textsf{rf}}\\
(\mathtt{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.8}snd}_{2}(\mathtt{ch}),\mathtt{\color[rgb]{0,0.4,0}rcv}_{2}(\mathtt{ch}))\in{\textsf{rf}}\\
\mathtt{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.8}snd}_{1}(\mathtt{ch})\neq\mathtt{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.8}snd}_{2}(\mathtt{ch})\end{array}\end{aligned}}{\begin{aligned} \begin{array}[]{c}(x_{\mathtt{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.8}snd}_{1}(\mathtt{ch})}<x_{\mathtt{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.8}snd}_{2}(\mathtt{ch})}\wedge x_{\mathtt{\color[rgb]{0,0.4,0}rcv}_{1}(\mathtt{ch})}<x_{\mathtt{\color[rgb]{0,0.4,0}rcv}_{2}(\mathtt{ch})})\vee\\
(x_{\mathtt{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.8}snd}_{1}(\mathtt{ch})}>x_{\mathtt{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.8}snd}_{2}(\mathtt{ch})}\wedge x_{\mathtt{\color[rgb]{0,0.4,0}rcv}_{1}(\mathtt{ch})}>x_{\mathtt{\color[rgb]{0,0.4,0}rcv}_{2}(\mathtt{ch})})\end{array}\end{aligned}}}

\psi^{unmatched}_{\textsf{FIFO}}=\bigwedge_{\mathtt{ch}\in\mathcal{C}}{\bigwedge_{\scriptsize\begin{aligned} \begin{array}[]{c}(\mathtt{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.8}snd}_{1}(\mathtt{ch}),\mathtt{\color[rgb]{0,0.4,0}rcv}_{1}(\mathtt{ch}))\in{\textsf{rf}}\\
\mathtt{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.8}snd}_{2}(\mathtt{ch})\;is\;unmatched\end{array}\end{aligned}}{\begin{aligned} \begin{array}[]{c}x_{\mathtt{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.8}snd}_{1}(\mathtt{ch})}<x_{\mathtt{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.8}snd}_{2}(\mathtt{ch})}\end{array}\end{aligned}}}

In other words,
\psi^{matched}_{\textsf{FIFO}}
requires that for every channel
\mathtt{ch}
, for every two distinct send/receive pairs
(\mathtt{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.8}snd}_{1}(\mathtt{ch}),\mathtt{\color[rgb]{0,0.4,0}rcv}_{1}(\mathtt{ch})),(\mathtt{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.8}snd}_{2}(\mathtt{ch}),\mathtt{\color[rgb]{0,0.4,0}rcv}_{2}(\mathtt{ch}))
,
either
\mathtt{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.8}snd}_{1}(\mathtt{ch})
is before
\mathtt{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.8}snd}_{2}(\mathtt{ch})
and
\mathtt{\color[rgb]{0,0.4,0}rcv}_{1}(\mathtt{ch})
is before
\mathtt{\color[rgb]{0,0.4,0}rcv}_{2}(\mathtt{ch})
,
or
\mathtt{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.8}snd}_{1}(\mathtt{ch})
is after
\mathtt{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.8}snd}_{2}(\mathtt{ch})
and
\mathtt{\color[rgb]{0,0.4,0}rcv}_{1}(\mathtt{ch})
is after
\mathtt{\color[rgb]{0,0.4,0}rcv}_{2}(\mathtt{ch})
.
This encoding exactly captures the
FIFO
property of channels.
Moreover
\psi^{unmatched}_{\textsf{FIFO}}
requires that for any channel
\mathtt{ch}
, all unmatched sends to
\mathtt{ch}
should be ordered after the matched sends to
\mathtt{ch}
.

\psi_{cap}=\psi^{cap}_{cap}\wedge\psi^{\mathtt{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.8}snd}}_{cap}\wedge\psi^{\mathtt{\color[rgb]{0,0.4,0}rcv}}_{cap}
,
where

\psi^{cap}_{cap}=\bigwedge_{0\leq i\leq n,\;\mathtt{ch}\in\mathcal{C}}{y^{\mathtt{\color[rgb]{0,0.4,0}rcv}}_{i}\leq y^{\mathtt{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.8}snd}}_{i}\leq y^{\mathtt{\color[rgb]{0,0.4,0}rcv}}_{i}+\mathsf{cap}(\mathtt{ch})}

\psi^{\mathtt{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.8}snd}}_{cap}=(\bigwedge_{\mathtt{ch}\in\mathcal{C}}{y^{\mathtt{ch}}_{\mathtt{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.8}snd},0}=0})\wedge(\bigwedge_{0\leq i\leq n-1,\mathtt{ch}\in\mathcal{C}}{\begin{aligned} \begin{array}[]{c}((\exists\mathtt{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.8}snd}(\mathtt{ch})\in\mathsf{S},x_{\mathtt{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.8}snd}(\mathtt{ch})}=i)\wedge y^{\mathtt{ch}}_{\mathtt{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.8}snd},i}+1=y^{\mathtt{ch}}_{\mathtt{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.8}snd},i+1})\vee\\
((\nexists\mathtt{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.8}snd}(\mathtt{ch})\in\mathsf{S},x_{\mathtt{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.8}snd}(\mathtt{ch})}=i)\wedge y^{\mathtt{ch}}_{\mathtt{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.8}snd},i}=y^{\mathtt{ch}}_{\mathtt{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.8}snd},i+1})\end{array}\end{aligned}})

\psi^{\mathtt{\color[rgb]{0,0.4,0}rcv}}_{cap}=(\bigwedge_{\mathtt{ch}\in\mathcal{C}}{y^{\mathtt{ch}}_{\mathtt{\color[rgb]{0,0.4,0}rcv},0}=0})\wedge(\bigwedge_{0\leq i\leq n-1,\mathtt{ch}\in\mathcal{C}}{\begin{aligned} \begin{array}[]{c}((\exists\mathtt{\color[rgb]{0,0.4,0}rcv}(\mathtt{ch})\in\mathsf{S},x_{\mathtt{\color[rgb]{0,0.4,0}rcv}(\mathtt{ch})}=i)\wedge y^{\mathtt{ch}}_{\mathtt{\color[rgb]{0,0.4,0}rcv},i}+1=y^{\mathtt{ch}}_{\mathtt{\color[rgb]{0,0.4,0}rcv},i+1})\vee\\
((\nexists\mathtt{\color[rgb]{0,0.4,0}rcv}(\mathtt{ch})\in\mathsf{S},x_{\mathtt{\color[rgb]{0,0.4,0}rcv}(\mathtt{ch})}=i)\wedge y^{\mathtt{ch}}_{\mathtt{\color[rgb]{0,0.4,0}rcv},i}=y^{\mathtt{ch}}_{\mathtt{\color[rgb]{0,0.4,0}rcv},i+1})\end{array}\end{aligned}})

\psi^{cap}_{cap}
explicitly encodes the capacity constraints,
i.e., at any prefix of a possible concretization, for any channel
\mathtt{ch}
, we require
num_{\mathtt{\color[rgb]{0,0.4,0}rcv}(\mathtt{ch})}\leq num_{\mathtt{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.8}snd}(\mathtt{ch})}\leq num_{\mathtt{\color[rgb]{0,0.4,0}rcv}(\mathtt{ch})}+\mathsf{cap}(\mathtt{ch})
,
where
num_{\mathtt{\color[rgb]{0,0.4,0}rcv}(\mathtt{ch})}
and
num_{\mathtt{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.8}snd}(\mathtt{ch})}
denote the number of receive/send events to
\mathtt{ch}
in this prefix.

\psi^{\mathtt{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.8}snd}}_{cap}
poses constraints on
y^{\mathtt{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.8}snd}}_{cap}
,
where we require (1)
y^{\mathtt{ch}}_{\mathtt{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.8}snd},0}
equals 0 for the prefix with no events, (2)
y^{\mathtt{ch}}_{\mathtt{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.8}snd},i}+1=y^{\mathtt{ch}}_{\mathtt{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.8}snd},i+1}
, if there exists a send to
\mathtt{ch}
whose position is
i
, and (3)
y^{\mathtt{ch}}_{\mathtt{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.8}snd},i}=y^{\mathtt{ch}}_{\mathtt{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.8}snd},i+1}
, if no send event to
\mathtt{ch}
is located at position
i
.
Similarly encoding
\psi^{\mathtt{\color[rgb]{0,0.4,0}rcv}}_{cap}
are also applied to
y^{\mathtt{ch}}_{\mathtt{\color[rgb]{0,0.4,0}rcv},i}
.

Now we show
\psi
is satisfiable iff
\langle\mathcal{X},\mathsf{cap},{\textsf{rf}}\rangle
is consistent.

Satisfiability
\Rightarrow
consistency.

Assuming
\psi
is satisfiable, then each event
e\in\mathsf{S}
must have been assigned a unique index
0\leq x_{e}\leq n-1
, as required by
\psi_{unique}
.
We claim that a valid concretization
\sigma
of
\mathcal{X}
can be obtained by ordering all events by their assigned integer variable, i.e. the
i
-th event in
\sigma
is the event
e
, s.t.
x_{e}=i
.
Clearly,
\sigma
satisfies program order, as in
\psi_{porf}
, we require every event to be ordered before their immediate thread successors.
Secondly,
\sigma
satisfies the capacity constraints for channels.
Following the encoding of
\psi^{\mathtt{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.8}snd}}_{cap}
and
\psi^{\mathtt{\color[rgb]{0,0.4,0}rcv}}_{cap}
, for any channel
\mathtt{ch}
,

y^{\mathtt{ch}}_{\mathtt{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.8}snd},i}
and
y^{\mathtt{ch}}_{\mathtt{\color[rgb]{0,0.4,0}rcv},i}
represent the number of send/receive events to
\mathtt{ch}
in the prefix
\pi
of
\sigma
, where
\pi
contains
i
events.
Then
\psi^{cap}_{cap}
explicitly ensures the capacity constraints.
Lastly, we show
{\textsf{rf}}_{\sigma}={\textsf{rf}}
.
For synchronous channels, the receive event is immediately after its matching send event, as required by
\psi^{rf\text{-}sync}_{porf}
.
Therefore, the reads-from constraints is satisfied.
For asynchronous channels,
\psi^{rf\text{-}async}_{porf}
guarantees every receive event is after its matching send event.
Moreover,
\psi^{unmatched}_{\textsf{FIFO}}
ensures for any channel
\mathtt{ch}
, every unmatched send to
\mathtt{ch}
is ordered after every match send event.
Finally,
\psi^{matched}_{\textsf{FIFO}}
explicitly encodes the
FIFO
property of all send/receive pairs for all channels.
Therefore, we conclude
\sigma
is indeed a valid concretization.

Consistency
\Rightarrow
satisfiability.

Now we show if
\langle\mathcal{X},\mathsf{cap},{\textsf{rf}}\rangle
is consistent, then
\psi
is satisfiable.
This direction is easier.
We take an arbitrary valid concretization
\sigma
of
\mathcal{X}
.
Based on
\sigma
, we assign
x_{e}=i
iff
e
is the
i
-th event in
\sigma
.
Moreover, we assign
y^{\mathtt{ch}}_{\mathtt{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.8}snd},i}=j
(
y^{\mathtt{ch}}_{\mathtt{\color[rgb]{0,0.4,0}rcv},i}=j
) iff there are
j
send (receive) events to
\mathtt{ch}
in the prefix
\pi
of
\sigma
,
where
\pi
contains
i
events.
Following the definition of valid concretization,
\psi
is obviously satisfied.

D.2.
Statistics of consistent/mutated instances

For each consistent instance,
we report the instance name (instance), consistency (cc), event number (
n
), thread number (
t
), channel number (
m
), maximal capacity (
k
) as well as the running time of each algorithm.
For algorithm that times out or run out of memory, the running time is set to be 10800s (the timeout value).
The details of statistics can be found via an artifact uploaded to
Zenodo
.
